# Gap analysis — Titanbot (Grok Bot 0.18 reconstruction)

**Measured 2026-09-02 on this Mac + the box `grok-bot-local-vm`, at commit `9875728`.** Ten audit
agents read five planes independently (dashboard surfaces, gateway coverage, connectors and plugins,
the model toolset, host stubs). Every finding was then handed to a second agent instructed to refute
it. 90 findings; 87 survived; 3 were refuted (two because the fix had already landed in `09753d6`
while the audit ran, one because its headline inference was wrong). The raw findings with verifier
notes live in the session record; the ids below (`MR-`, `GW-`, `CP-`, `TOOLS-`, `SP-`, `BL-`) are
stable so a wave can cite them.

This document is the ranked backlog. `docs/PLUMBING-AUDIT.md` §5 remains the live open-problems
ledger for the *host* and now points here; the living dashboard carries the current position.

## 0. Status, 2026-09-03 03:00 CDT (measured on this Mac + the box, bundle from the working tree after `526a013`)

**Waves A and B landed** (`526a013`, plus the browser-subagent follow-up commit). Every proof in
§5 for those two waves ran green on the live box in one sequential pass: unit 126/126, source
typecheck, `verify-toolset` in all four modes (chief 36 tools with GetMcpTools and CallMcpTool, 35 once CLOUD-1 withheld CloudAgent;
computerUse child 3 tools; connector round trip stamped evidenced; browserUse child reads
example.com and the parent reports "Example Domain"), subagent dispatch, evidence replay, work
report with evidence required, dashboard 35 checks plus its leak arm, subscription leak scan.

**Landed rows** are marked "landed" in §4. Left open from those waves, now their own rows:

| Id | Finding | Next | Size |
|---|---|---|---|

| ENDPOINT-2 | The relay writes the `SAND_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_*` pin into `box-secrets.json`, a store whose `SAND_` prefix is reserved, so `BoxSecretsApplier.applyPersisted` has always bailed on this box and no real box secret is ever injected | Move the pin to `sand-host-settings.json` in `provider-session.ts` and repoint `ui/server.mjs` at the same file in one change. Proof: `box-secrets.json` holds no `SAND_` key and the host log prints "applying N persisted box secret(s)" | M |
| GW-15 · **landed** | `setAgentUnread{isUnread:true}` errors with "this.tm.sessionStore.seedSessionActivityFromDbMtime is not a function"; the clear direction works, which is all MR-06 needs | Trace the session store method on the raise path; one host fix | S |
| MR-11, MR-15 · **landed** | The run rail's synthetic timeline, and the credential card | Both landed in D1: the rail shows the turn's tool rows in order; the secret request renders as its own masked card answered through `submitSecret`. Sweep 2026-09-04 measured both live | S / M |

**Wave D1 landed 2026-09-03 (Opus workflow, host and dashboard in parallel, each adversarially
verified then reviewed and fixed; the full gate set re-run by the orchestrator).** The connector
plane now runs on its own stdio spine. CP-10: a submitted connector secret goes into a host-owned
`connector-env-secrets.json` (0600, written temp-file plus rename) and into that server's process
env at spawn, never into `connectors.json`, never into a per-agent store, never into a log; the
gateway gains `setConnectorSecret`, `deleteConnectorSecret` and `listConnectorSecretFields`
(names only); process-control env names are refused; the chat platforms (slack, github) win the
shared namespace so a channel token can never land in a connector process. CP-07: local
connectors get stable numeric ids derived from their name, persisted beside `connectors.json`, so
instructions, per-tool toggles and authenticate reach them for the first time. CP-08:
`listMcpServerTools` and `toggleMcpToolDisabled`; a disabled tool vanishes from routed tools and
from GetMcpTools. CP-03: `listInstalledMcpServers`, `listMcpPlugins`, `getMcpPlugin`. CP-05
short term: the catalog path answers empty instead of throwing at the model. CP-12: disconnect
requires the agent id. Dashboard: connector cards from the installed list with real per-tool
switches, a connectors editor on the relay route with `refreshMcp` after a write, a key form per
connector, the masked secret-request card answered through `submitSecret`, listener Connect
through a local token form and a labelled Cursor link, Disconnect with the agent id, and the run
rail shows the turn's tool rows (MR-11). The relay answers 503 when the box cannot be read instead
of an empty map, and the installed-server id is a number on the wire. New gate:
`scripts/verify-connector-plane.mjs`.

**Wave T landed 2026-09-04 (teach by demonstration; Opus workflow, host then dashboard, each
adversarially verified with fix rounds, two review lenses, a review fix; the full gate set re-run
by the orchestrator).** Learn was never a stub: it was real code behind a Statsig gate this box can
never bootstrap, and its stop-and-save needed a managed skill only Cursor's dashboard hands out.
Host: `SAND_TEACH` in the host settings file resolves the gate the way `SAND_BROWSER_USE` does,
read live on every start, and the `[sand][gates]` line names it as the source. The two real
managed skills from the August backup (`learn-from-demonstration`, `add-connector`) are baked
into the bundle as seeds; every managed-skills cache write is the union of seeds and fetched
(fetched wins on an id), and `ensureSeeds` repairs `cache.json` and `skills/<id>/SKILL.md` by
content at start (a hand-mangled row and a deleted file were both repaired live, measured by
mutation). The learning turn arrives whole: `WORKFLOW_INJECTED_BODY_LIMIT` is raised from the
shipped bundle's 8000 to 16000 (a knowing divergence, noted at the constant; the product truncates
its own 9985-character recipe), the teach queue scope is injected, and one `[sand][workflow]` line
per invocation records how much of the recipe the model was handed. Gate
`scripts/verify-teach.mjs`, ten asserting legs on the live box plus an eleventh for ownership that may go inconclusive (GATE-8): refused with the gate off; recording on the
probe's own display with ffmpeg writing `demo.mp4`; discard leaves no session and no queue entry;
save writes `session.json` and one signed queue entry and dispatches the learning turn carrying the
whole recipe and the scope; the agent claimed the queue file within about two minutes on the box's
live model. Dashboard: the modal opens only when the host confirms a recording, the reason sits
beside the Learn button when it cannot (gate off, or the host's own message); the screen starts as a
cover so the dialog keeps the keyboard, and a click connects the live desktop; Discard and Finish
both call the host, are disabled while the call is in flight, and show a host error inside the
dialog; a status poll every five seconds closes the dialog when the box ends the recording on its
own and says that the cap is a save; the operator's note goes as an ordinary message once the
learning turn is already dispatched, and the copy says the recipe does not read it; the button is
"Learn this task"; the frame lays out its footer inside the dialog at three viewports. Gate
`scripts/verify-dashboard.mjs --teach`. Also landed in this pass: DISPLAY-4 (below), a sweep of
stale prompt-trace reports, sqlite3 in the box for the recipe's history step. Left open, own rows
below: TEACH-2, TEACH-3, DISPLAY-5.

**Closure sweep 2026-09-04 05:28 CDT (Opus workflow: one verifier per plane, one skeptic per plane
attacking every row called good, one completeness critic; 13 agents, read-mostly on the box,
this morning's 20-gate log as the evidence for the heavy gates).** 98 rows measured: 75 landed
rows hold on the box today; 4 open rows had been fixed along the way (MR-11, MR-15, GW-07,
TOOLS-11, corrected above); 18 stay open (each row's next action rewritten from the measurement);
1 landed row regressed (REPORTS-1: the report of an agent deleted while active survives; fix in
flight). The skeptics overturned five verifier calls, recorded on their rows: SUB-2 proved the
render and not the wire (SUB-2b), DISPLAY-1's loader still admits the key "undefined"
(DISPLAY-1b), GW-07 landed by a different fix than the row named, GW-04's residual was obsolete,
and one P2 evidence anchor was wrong. The critic found eleven things no row named; the two that
block the promise are the same shape Learn was: a Statsig gate this box can never bootstrap.
Twelve cheap fixes from the sweep landed the same morning (REPORTS-1, TOOLS-12, WORKFLOW-1,
SP-3, FLAGS-2, TOOLS-16, the Now island's invented copy that painted for a second before the
host answered, DISPLAY-1b, the toolset gate now selecting the wire line by conversation id and
asserting offered equals sent equals the toolset, two gate comments, and GW-16 with the real
takeover path). SUB-2b turned out to be a product defect, not a gate gap: the history flattener
dropped every rendered screenshot before the request left (2 in history, 0 on the wire); fixed
by emitting the image as an image part after its tool result, the way the transport's own path
does, and the browser gate now fails if a rendered image does not leave. The rest are rows below.

**Wave S landed 2026-09-04 06:25 CDT (Opus workflow: scout, implementer, verifier, one fix round,
verifier).** The install is in `deploy/r750/` (README with the operator checklist). Measured on
the R750: the box boots the pinned image digest with the fresh token and the placeholder
credential, the gateway answers `getHostStatus` two seconds after start, the relay injects the
bearer, the Machine Room renders over the tailnet in real Chrome, a probe agent is created and
deleted three times, install re-runs are idempotent, uninstall keeps the volumes and a reinstall
adopts them. Operator steps left, in the README: the DNS-01 resolver on Coolify's `letsencrypt`
(needs a Cloudflare token scoped to DNS edit), `enable-route.sh` for the domain, an off-tailnet
request to prove the allowlist, `ui/endpoints.json` with an API-key endpoint (no model until then),
the skills restore. Found on the way and filed: AUTH-1, VNC-1, SOCKET-1. Jason's first hour on it (06:40) found three more: the typing indicator never stopped (ENDPOINT-3 with the classifier guard, both landed), the desktop frame reached for his own Mac (VNC-2, queued behind the login), and the first agent was named Grok (renamed Titan, and the default is now Titan).

| CONNECT-13 · **found by MARKET-6, 2026-09-08** | `connectors.ids.json` only ever grows. Every connector name the box has ever seen keeps a numeric id in it forever, including every name a gate invented for one arm and removed a minute later, and nothing prunes it. **Measured on grok-bot-local-vm 2026-09-08:** 36 names, of which 30 are dead — `stalled-*` from the unreachable-connector arm, `mktprobe-*`, `chostprobe-*`, `probetx*`, `probe-u3-*`, `gateprobe`, `gateremote`, `gateargv`, `writeprobe`, `revssrf1` — against 3 entries actually in `connectors.json`. It is not a leak of anything secret and it is not big; it is that the file is the box's map from a name to the numeric id the older tool surface addresses a server by, so a name that comes back later inherits an id from a run nobody remembers, and a reader looking for what this box has connected reads mostly rubble. The same run left a live `envprobe` entry in `connectors.json` pointing at `/workspace/mcp-env-probe.mjs`, which does not exist, so the box carries a connector that can only ever fail | Two halves, and the cheap one first. (1) Gates clean up after themselves: every arm that mints a name removes its id as well as its entry, which is a line in the teardown each of them already has. (2) The host drops an id whose name is in neither `connectors.json` nor the catalog when it next writes the file, which is the only place that knows both. Do NOT prune on read — an id that disappears while a tool is holding it is worse than an id nobody uses. Owner: the next connector pass; next action: (1), because it stops the growth without touching the host | S |
| MARKET-14 · **found and closed by MARKET-6, 2026-09-08** | The Add-your-own picker has offered two transports since the card shipped — "Streamable HTTP" and "SSE (older; only if the server's docs say so)" — and nothing had ever gone through the second one. Every gate leg drove the first: `verify-marketplace`'s add leg asserts `transport === "http"`, and `verify-connector-host`'s spike and custody arms are both `type: "http"`. So a door a customer can pick was shipped on the assumption that the box would do the right thing with it. The two are different protocols on the wire, not a version flag — the older one answers a GET with a stream, names a POST address in its first event, and returns every answer on the stream rather than in the POST's response — so a fallback to the modern transport does not degrade, it fails, and would look identical on the page. There was no vendor to prove it against either: no catalog row and no researched candidate recommends SSE, and the obvious public endpoint answers 410 on its `/sse` | Closed by proving it against a server this repository owns rather than a vendor's retirement schedule. `scripts/lib/mcp-bearer-stub.mjs` serves the older transport under `MCP_STUB_SSE=1`, both modes answering out of one handler so a difference between them can only be transport; `tests/mcp-stub-sse.test.mjs` pins the server's half (7 cases: the `endpoint` event and its session, answers on the stream with the POST settling 202 and empty, the bearer required on BOTH the stream and the messages, an unknown session refused, neither mode serving the other's route, and the two transports returning byte-identical answers); and `scripts/verify-marketplace.mjs --sse` runs that stub inside a box, adds it through the picker's second option, and requires the row back as `transport=sse` with the far end's two tools listed and the stored value in no process argument list. **Measured:** the unit half on this Mac, 7/7. The box half is an integration arm and is named in the run log with its verdict | S |
| MARKET-15 · **found by MARKET-6, closed by its review pass, 2026-09-08 (bundle `e6a2c5d38993`)** | A connector could be pointed at the box's own control plane. The rule refused loopback and link-local and nothing else, so the box's own address on the docker bridge went through — and that address is the SAME gateway on 1340 and the same exec daemons on 1337/1338. **Measured from inside the R750 demo box, 2026-09-08:** `POST http://192.168.48.6:1340/api/getHostStatus` with that box's own `SAND_GATEWAY_TOKEN` answered `200 {"hostVersion":"4a4eda387ead",…}`, byte-identical to the same POST against `127.0.0.1:1340`; `192.168.48.6:1337` answered HTTP too; `ss -ltn` showed 1337, 1338, 1339 and 1340 all bound to `0.0.0.0`. `addLocalConnector` with `url http://192.168.48.6:1341/mcp` and a secret `Authorization` header was ACCEPTED while the same call on `127.0.0.1` was refused. The docker default gateway `192.168.32.1:80` — the machine's own proxy — answered 404, i.e. is reachable. **The row's original cross-tenant claim was wrong and is withdrawn:** from the demo box, `192.168.48.3:1340`, `192.168.48.3:1337` and `192.168.64.2:1340` (Jason's and Richard's boxes) all failed to connect, curl code 000. Box isolation holds; what the rule handed out was the box's own control plane and its host's services. | **Closed.** `localRemoteUrlRefusal` now refuses the whole private space (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 169.254/16, 100.64/10, 0/8, fc00::/7, fe80::/10 and the `.local`/`.internal`/`.home.arpa` names), with `allowPrivateNetwork` stated on the entry as the one way in — settable only through a direct gateway call, which takes the box's own token; neither console door nor the agent's `AddMcpServer` carries the field, and loopback stays refused even with it. Both gates that point a connector at an in-box stub now say so: `verify-connector-host`'s spike and custody arms and `verify-marketplace --sse`. **Measured on grok-bot-local-vm, bundle from this tree:** the box's own bridge address, the docker default gateway and the box's own stub without the opt-in are all refused in one sentence; the same stub WITH the opt-in connects and lists its two tools; 12 refusals changed no byte of connectors.json. `tests/connector-custody.test.mjs` and `tests/connector-plane.test.mjs` pin the table, including that the opt-in never reaches loopback | M |
| MARKET-16 · **found by MARKET-6, 2026-09-08** | A key typed into a header the allowlist does not recognise is written into `connectors.json` in the clear. Both doors test the header's NAME against the same regex, byte-identical in two files (`local-connectors.ts:280`, `sand-mcp-management-tools.ts:206`), and it covers `authorization`, `cookie`, `api-key`, `x-api-key`, `x-auth-token`, `x-access-token` and the `x-<vendor>-(key|token|secret)` shape. **Measured on this Mac, 2026-09-08, against the merged tip:** `Authorization`, `X-Api-Key`, `Cookie` and `X-Figma-Token` carrying `glpat-…` are all refused by both doors; **`Private-Token` carrying the same value is ALLOWED by both**, and so is `PRIVATE-TOKEN`. That is GitLab's own header, written exactly as GitLab's docs write it, and GitLab is not an exotic vendor. Nothing in the catalog uses it, so nothing shipped is broken by it — the hole is for the server the operator adds themselves, which is the whole point of the Add-your-own door | The name test is the wrong shape to be exhaustive in, but broadening it is safe in the direction that matters: a false positive only asks the operator to put the value in the masked box, a false negative puts a key in a file that is listed, copied and backed up in the clear. So broaden, and broaden in ONE place — the two regexes are the same string in two files and will drift. Not done here because it is a change to what every connector add on every live box accepts, including a real customer's, and it belongs with MARKET-15 rather than as a lone regex edit; `3e643c9` carries the same fix with its test. Owner: the next connector pass. Next action: move the pattern into one exported constant both doors import, add `private-token` and the bare `<vendor>-token` shape, and pin the list in a test that names the vendor each entry is for | S |
| MARKET-17 · **found by MARKET-6, closed by its review pass, 2026-09-08 (bundle `e6a2c5d38993`)** | The argv leak was closed for NEW entries and nothing rewrote the old ones, so a real customer's key was still on a command line. MARKET-7 changed what the writer produces; all three R750 boxes were handed their bridged TinyFish entry long before that, and the box's exec daemon expands `${TINYFISH_API_KEY}` into `--header "Authorization:Bearer …"` before it execs. **Measured read-only on the R750, 2026-09-08, before the fix:** on each of `titanbot-box-p927bfqm83ioloibamlvyd7g` (Jason's), `titanbot-box-wepegxhh3fpvr83bubvz5xm5` (Richard's) and `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau` (the demo), `connectors.json` held `tinyfish = {"command":"npx","args":["-y","mcp-remote@0.8.3","https://agent.tinyfish.ai/mcp","--header","Authorization:Bearer ${TINYFISH_API_KEY}"]}` and the one stored value (44 chars, sha256 `9165ce2daa86`, the SAME key on all three) was in **3 process argument lists each**, readable by `ps -eo args` from the agent's own root shell. | **Closed, in two parts, the second found by measuring the first.** (1) `migrateBridgedRemoteEntries` rewrites a bridged remote into the link shape at host start and again whenever the server list is rebuilt: url, `--transport` and each `--header` become `{type, url, headers}`, a placeholder is carried across untouched, a literal key is moved into the 0600 store first and the entry is left bridged with a logged reason if it cannot be, and an entry already on a private address keeps that reach. Idempotent. (2) **The file rewrite alone changed nothing on a running box** — measured minutes after the first swap on the demo box: the entry was the link shape and the same three `npx mcp-remote` processes were still there, 29 hours old, because the daemon stops a server only when its NAME leaves the list it is pushed. So once per host start every connector that is now a link is stopped and started (pushed absent, then pushed back), which costs one request per link and cannot leave a process from an older shape running. **Measured on the R750 after the ship, all three boxes:** entry `tinyfish` = link http, stored value in **0** process argument lists (428, 176 and 211 processes read), and the connector itself `status=connected transport=http tools=16 "Working. It offers 16 tools."` on each. Host log: `link connectors restarted once at start (tinyfish)`. See MARKET-24 for the rotation this key still needs | M |
| MARKET-18 · **found by MARKET-6, closed by its review pass, 2026-09-08** | The OAuth refusal the card advertises almost never fired. MARKET-13 concluded that an OAuth-protected remote cannot be authorised on this build without a person at that box's own browser, and the card was supposed to refuse one at the door. What it tested was a literal `"auth": "oauth"` key in a pasted config block, which no vendor writes — and `grep -n oauth ui/machine-room/app.js` returns nothing, so no control in the console ever sets it. The sentence it would have said was also untrue: it told the operator to sign in on the box's desktop and add the server again, and there is no OAuth code path on either the link or the bridge, so the second add returns the same sentence forever. | **Closed.** The sentence is now the true one — a browser sign-in is a named non-goal, and most such servers also publish an API key — and the console and the host say the SAME string, pinned by a test that reads the adapter's source against the host's constant. The host reaches it by asking the endpoint, and only when the operator supplied no key at all: one unauthenticated `initialize`, and a 401 whose `WWW-Authenticate` names an OAuth realm or resource metadata is a browser sign-in (`https://mcp.notion.com/mcp`, measured), while a plain key challenge is not (`https://mcp.zapier.com/api/mcp/mcp` answers `realm="Zapier MCP", error="invalid_token"`, measured, and that server takes a key). A network failure refuses nothing, so a box with a flaky egress path can still add a server. Behind the add door only, never in the writer, so a slow endpoint delays a form and can never block a host start. Pinned in `tests/connector-custody.test.mjs` with the measured challenges as fixtures; the non-goal is written out in docs/CONNECTORS.md | S |
| MARKET-20 · **found and closed by MARKET-6's integration, 2026-09-08** | The Add-your-own link door could not produce a working bearer header. A secret header was written as the bare placeholder — `Authorization: ${FIELD}` — so a person who pasted an address, left the header row on Authorization, ticked it secret and typed their key into the masked box afterwards got a header with no scheme on it. The far end answers 401 and the health line then says the server refused their key, which sends them to look at the key, which is fine. There was no way to reach a working bearer header through the form at all. Every catalog row that authenticates with `Authorization` writes `Bearer ${FIELD}`, and every row that uses a vendor's own header (`x-api-key`, `X-Browser-Use-API-Key`) writes it bare, because that is what those servers ask for; the form agreed with neither. It was invisible until a gate arm drove a real far end: it is what made the older-transport connector refuse to connect. **Closed.** `byoHeaderPlaceholder` in `gateway-adapter.js` writes `Bearer` and the placeholder for `Authorization` (case-insensitively) and the placeholder bare for every other header, the preview shows the scheme it is about to write, and `tests/machine-room-byo-mcp.test.mjs` pins all three cases — bearer, vendor header, and a non-secret literal — so getting it wrong the other way would break Exa and Browser Use loudly. **Measured on grok-bot-local-vm, bundle 63da28b2:** with the scheme, the older-transport connector reached `connected` with the far end's two tools; without it, `That address is not a connectable endpoint any more` | S |
| MARKET-21 · **found by MARKET-6's integration, 2026-09-08** | The console's door and the host's door disagree about which addresses are allowed, and only the console's half is written down. `byoUrlProblem` in `gateway-adapter.js` refuses any non-https address and any private, loopback or link-local host; the host accepts plain http on a private network and accepts a private host outright (which is MARKET-15). So there are addresses a person cannot add through the console that the agent's own tools and the gateway command will take. That is the safe direction to disagree in, and it is not the problem. The problem is that nothing says so: the first version of `verify-marketplace`'s older-transport arm filled the console form with the address of a stub inside the box and could never have passed, and it took a run to find that out rather than a sentence in a doc. | **The address half is closed** (MARKET-15, 2026-09-08): the host's rule is now the tighter one, with `allowPrivateNetwork` on the entry as the explicit opt-in the two in-box gates use, and docs/CONNECTORS.md states both doors' rules side by side. What is left is the header-name half, MARKET-16, which is still open. Next action: land MARKET-16, then this row closes with it | S |
| MARKET-22 · **found and closed by MARKET-6's review pass, 2026-09-08** | Every address rule in the tree could be walked around by writing the address in its IPv6 coat, because all three validators tested the TEXT of the hostname. **Measured against the live gateway on the R750 demo box, 2026-09-08, by calling `addLocalConnector` with a secret `Authorization` header:** `https://[::ffff:127.0.0.1]:1341/mcp` ACCEPTED, `https://[::]:1341/mcp` ACCEPTED, `https://[::ffff:169.254.169.254]/mcp` ACCEPTED, while `https://127.0.0.1:1341/mcp` was refused. The console's `byoPrivateHost` had the same blind spot (`byoPrivateHost("::ffff:7f00:1") === false`, evaluated out of the shipped file on this Mac) and so did `remoteMcpUrlProblem` in the shared spec. Every probe entry was removed and both store files ended byte-identical | **Closed.** One `normalizedHostname` in `source/shared/marketplace/connector-spec.ts`, imported by the host's writer and mirrored in the console's own copy (that file has no imports): brackets and a zone id come off, a v4-mapped or v4-compatible address becomes its dotted quad, any other v6 literal becomes its full eight-group form, and the v6 range tests are then exactly fc00::/7 and fe80::/10 rather than whatever starts with the same two letters. Pinned in three suites (`connector-spec`, `connector-custody`, `machine-room-byo-mcp`) with the four measured cases plus `[0:0:0:0:0:ffff:7f00:1]`, and as three refusal legs in `verify-connector-host`, which measured them refused on grok-bot-local-vm. A global-unicast v6 server (`https://[2606:4700::1111]/mcp`) stays addable at every door | S |
| MARKET-23 · **found and closed by MARKET-6's review pass, 2026-09-08** | "Uninstall removes the entry and offers to clear its secrets" was true at one of three doors. **Measured on the R750 demo box, 2026-09-08:** `removeLocalConnector {server:"mkt6rev-ssrf"}` — exactly what the plugin card's Remove sends and what the relay's connector-editor delete sends — answered `{removed:true, cleared:[], stored:["MKT6REV_TOKEN"]}`, and `listConnectorSecretOrphans` then named the orphan; the same call with `clearSecrets:true` answered `cleared:["MKT6REV_TOKEN"]` and the orphan list went empty. | SHIP-2 · **landed** (2026-09-06, `74bcc6a`, measured on the Mac box; part 3 is still open, see SHIP-4) | A host ship recreates both containers, which cuts every turn in flight and wipes what agents installed into the container outside the volumes: at 21:15 Titan lost the Bitwarden CLI, its session key and his GitHub auth ("my computer restarted around 2:15 and wiped non-persistent state"); the operator does not want working agents reset by an update R750: armed 2026-09-06 15:40 (bundle URL at the relay, auto-update flags); the first in-place ship is the next host change. | Part (1) split out as PERSIST-1 and landed. Part (2) landed: the host already carried upstream's self-upgrade (`<base>/sand-host-bundle-latest.version` then `<base>/sand-host-bundle-<version>.tgz`, staged for the in-box supervisor, which swaps the bundle and relaunches ONLY the host process) and nothing had ever pointed `SAND_HOST_BUNDLE_S3_BASE_URL` anywhere but Cursor's S3 bucket. The relay now serves that layout out of the ship's runtime directory at `/runtime/<gateway token>/…` — before the console's login, because the caller is the box's own `fetch` and can hold neither a cookie nor a header, so the bearer is the path segment, compared in constant time, and a wrong one is `404` rather than `401`; `sync.sh` stages the version file beside the bundle (`scripts/stage-host-bundle.mjs`), `common.sh` and the compose file mount that directory into the relay read-only. **Two things were read out of the image rather than designed.** `swapHostBundle` in `/usr/local/bin/sand-supervisor.mjs` PRUNES every entry of `/home/box/sand-host` the archive did not carry, so a tarball holding only `host-main.cjs` would delete `box-scripts`, `extensions`, `node_modules`, the pdf and diff workers and `sand-eval-runner.cjs` — the parts of that tree this repo does not build; the relay therefore composes the archive INSIDE the box from the box's own tree, replacing the two entries a ship owns, so the base always matches the image about to receive the swap. And the swap ends in `renameSync` onto `/home/box/sand-host/host-main.cjs`, which cannot rename over a mount point, so the bundle is now COPIED in at container start (the two-line entrypoint the Coolify file already used) instead of bind-mounted. One host fix came with it: the latest-version lookup is cached 10 min, right for the daily watch and wrong for an explicit ship — a second ship inside that window answered `already-latest` and did nothing (measured twice before the cause was found); `updateHostNow` now drops the cache first, the watch still keeps it. `SAND_BOX_AUTO_UPDATE=1` arms the host-bundle watch at upstream's daily interval AND, by the same flag, disables the image auto-update that would recreate the container. There are THREE image-update entry points, not two, and all three are now gated on it: the ensure path (`maybeAutoUpdate`, `forever-box-service.ts:91`), the daily image watch (`watchForImageUpdate`, `:95`), and the gateway's `autoUpdateBoxNow` (`autoUpdateNow`, `:83`). The third one carried only the `autoUpdateEnabled` gate. It answered `auto-update-disabled` for as long as `SAND_BOX_AUTO_UPDATE` was 0, and this wave, which set that flag to 1 and added `SAND_BOX_STORE_COPY_IN=1`, is what would have armed it for the first time; no in-repo caller exists yet, so it was latent, but the command is on the gateway and its success path is `requestRecreate({preserveData:true})`. It now refuses with `host-bundle-auto-update`, the same way the other two do. **Measured, green twice in a row** (`scripts/verify-host-upgrade.mjs`, the second run with no wait): the box read the version file over its own base URL, a wrong token segment got 404, `updateHostNow` staged `c03c88a2c20e` then `3d3dd84fa48e`, the host pid went 6892 → 20972 → 24292, and across both swaps the container id and `StartedAt` never changed, Chief of staff's desktop stayed the same Xvfb pid 5116, the roster stayed four agents, and a shell secret set before each swap was still set after it. **Owed on the R750:** the next `install.sh` (it recreates the box, which is what picks up the new entrypoint and the base URL) | M |
| PERSIST-1 · **landed** (2026-09-06, `1f2f3eb`, measured on the Mac box) | SHIP-2 part (1), split out because it is its own mechanism. The box store sync has mirrored `/home/box/cli-config` and the three volumes to the box-store volume on every cycle for as long as this deployment has existed, and nothing has ever restored it: the image's `/usr/local/bin/start-sand-box` runs `host-main.cjs --box-copy-in` only when `SAND_BOX_STORE_COPY_IN` is truthy AND a host bundle is already in place, and the recreate script, the compose file and `install.sh` all set `SAND_BOX_STORE_SYNC` while none of them set COPY_IN. So every recreate came up with whatever the image ships, and the agents' CLI logins, git identity and `~/.config` settings went with it — the container's own filesystem is on no volume R750: the one-time recreate ran 2026-09-06 15:40 (copy-in hydrated 1327 files, roster intact). | Set `SAND_BOX_STORE_COPY_IN=1` in all three deploy paths. **Measured** (`scripts/verify-persistence.mjs`, which recreates the box and therefore takes `/tmp/titanbot-box.lock` itself): markers in `cli-config`, `~/.config` and `sand-data` all reached the store manifest, the container was replaced (`e319f33ad6d2` → `b0da1aecba2f`), copy-in reported `{"phase":"done","restored":1386,"total":1386,"bytes":251298815,"outcome":"hydrated"}`, all three markers read back, and the roster was the same four agents. **Two facts the design did not say, measured here.** `~/.config` does NOT reach the store directly — the box-home category is built only under `SAND_STORE_BETTER_CLI=1` and `SAND_USER_NON_ROOT=1` (`box-store-sync-service.ts`), and no deployment sets either — it gets there through the image's own `persist-cli-auth`, which mirrors each `~/.config/<tool>` plus `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.docker`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.npmrc`, `~/.gitconfig` into `cli-config` every 30 s. And the copy-in needs no inference credential on these deployments: `SAND_BOX_STORE_LOCAL_DIR` puts the store on `local-fs`, and the token getter is only built for the remote backends. **Owed on the R750:** one recreate, because the variable is read at container start. **Still open:** `~/.local`, `~/.cache` and the pip user site are in no category at all, so a `pip install --user` still does not survive — see PERSIST-2 | S |
| PERSIST-3 | What survives a recreate is what `persist-cli-auth` mirrors into `cli-config` every 30 s: `.config/gh`, `.aws`, `.config/gcloud`, `.ssh`, `.docker`, `.vercel`, `.fly`, `.config/fly`, `.netrc`, `.npmrc`, `.gitconfig`, `.git-credentials`, plus a sweep of `~/.config/<name>` gated by `CLI_AUTH_CONFIG_SWEEP=1`; on the R750 (2026-09-06 15:40) a marker directory under `~/.config` did NOT come back, so the sweep's rule is unverified and Bitwarden's `~/.config/Bitwarden CLI` may not persist | owner: the next persistence pass; next action: read the sweep's rule in the image, prove Bitwarden's directory round-trips on the Mac box, and if it does not, add it to the target list through the box's own mechanism | M |
| PERSIST-2 | `~/.local`, `~/.cache` and the pip user site are covered by no box-store category and no `persist-cli-auth` target, so a tool an agent installs with `pip install --user` or `npm i -g --prefix ~/.local` is gone on the next recreate. The category that would cover them (`buildBoxHomeCategory`, `/home/box` minus the guarded trees) exists and is gated on `SAND_STORE_BETTER_CLI=1` AND `SAND_USER_NON_ROOT=1` together | Decide whether the box runs as a non-root user, which is what the second flag means and is a much larger change than the first: today the agent's shell is root and every file in `sand-data` is root-owned. The cheaper alternative is a fifth docker volume mounted at `/home/box/.local`, which needs no store category and no user change, and covers the pip case exactly. Either way it wants its own measurement, not a flag flip | M |
| BACKUP-1 · **landed** (2026-09-06, `206d34f`, measured on the Mac box) | There is no backup job of any kind on the R750. An instance is five places — the `workspace`, `sand-data`, box-store and `chrome-profile` volumes and the relay side (`ui/auth.json`, `ui/endpoints.json`, `ui/subscriptions.json`, `profile/`, `credential/`) — and a lost volume is a lost instance: every agent, every transcript, every credential R750: timer 04:11 nightly to /mnt/rosa-storage/archives/titanbot/backups; first snapshot 2026-09-06 15:47, 1.6 GB, box paused 2 s, drill OK on both stores (after two fixes: the box found by label, the stream using the box's own image, the relay owning its files). | `deploy/backup/snapshot.sh` copies all five into `<dest>/<instance>/<YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM>/` with a manifest carrying sizes and the sha256 of every `store.db`, and keeps the last 14; `deploy/backup/restore-drill.sh` restores one into a throwaway directory and opens every store with `PRAGMA integrity_check`, re-hashing each against the manifest so damage done to a snapshot AFTER it was taken reads differently from a torn copy. Two things a skeptic pass found and this row now describes. The drill compares each store against the hash recorded for THAT path, not against the whole manifest (the loose form passed two stores swapped between agent directories, because both hashes were still present), and it refuses when the number of stores it opened is not the `storeDbCount` the manifest names, so a snapshot whose paused re-copy half-failed cannot come back green on the one agent it kept. And retention never sweeps the newest snapshot whose manifest says `"mode": "consistent"`: the sweep evicted strictly by name, a `live` snapshot and a torn run with no manifest at all each took a slot, and with `KEEP=14` and a nightly timer fourteen degraded runs in a row would have deleted the last real restore point one night at a time. Two guards refuse rather than warn: the destination must be a mount point (an unmounted `/mnt/rosa-storage` is an ordinary directory, and a nightly job would put the only copy of the data on the disk it exists to survive) and there must be room for twice the last snapshot. **The pause is one second, not the length of the copy**: a full freeze would be minutes of dead instance nightly (2.9 GB on the dev box, 2.6 GB of it the box store), so everything is copied live and then `sand-data` and `workspace` — the volumes holding the sqlite stores, the only files that can tear — are retaken with the box paused; every source carries its own `capturedWhile` and the snapshot is `"mode":"consistent"` only when that pass ran. `install.sh` installs a systemd USER timer at 04:10 (the install runs as `sem` with no sudo) and prints the cron line when there is no `systemd --user`. **Measured on the Mac box under the lock**, against the live volumes: 2.9 GB copied, paused 1 s, mode consistent, 5 `store.db` recorded, the drill opened all five with `integrity_check ok` and every hash matching, roster unchanged. The first drill run failed 4 of 5 and was right to be believed: these are WAL databases and one opened read-only with no `-shm` beside it cannot create one, so the drill opens its throwaway copy read-write. **Owed on the R750:** run `install.sh` (it installs the units) and then `loginctl enable-linger sem`, or the timer only fires while `sem` has a session. A Mac equivalent is documented in the runbook and deliberately NOT installed | M |

| SECRET-1 · **landed** (2026-09-06, bundle cc1c998c) | The original product answers an agent's request for a credential with a masked card inline in the chat ("Titan Job Bus token", "Never share in chat", a password field, **Save securely**, then **Saved securely and kept private.** with a green ✓ Saved pill) and the value lands as an environment variable of that agent's box; the card says "it'll land as env `TITAN_JOB_TOKEN` for this box". Every piece of that was on this box already and none of them met: the `secret-request` message type existed, the console's masked card existed, `submitSecret` → `routeSecret` existed, and the CONNECT-5 shell store IS the agent's box shell environment, but `routeSecret` had exactly three destinations (a local connector's process env, the slack/github channel store, the per-agent channel file) and **no route to the agent's own shell**. The missing piece was a name | The reserved connector name `"shell"` in a secret-request means the agent's own box shell, and `field` is then the environment variable name. `routeSecret` returns before the connector and channel branches (a shell request must never fall through to a store the agent can read back) into a new `tm.shellSecretSink`, wired in `sand-host.ts` beside `connectorSecretSink`, which does `writeShellEnvSecret` plus the same box push `setShellSecret` uses and answers `{stored, applied}`; the ack the model reads is true for the new destination: "It is set in your shell's environment as $FIELD; commands you run from now on see it (applied: yes/no)". One field rule for both writers (`shell-secret-field.ts`, shared with `requireShellField`): UPPERCASE, with process control, the shell's own `HOME`/`PWD`/`USER` and the host's `SAND_*` refused; a refused name is caught in the tool schema so the card is never drawn, and if one gets through nothing is stored anywhere. Console copy is the product's, in both themes, and the value never enters the markup or the transcript. Measured: `tests/secret-request-shell.test.mjs` 6 cases, 671 unit tests green, `verify-connector-plane --shell-secrets` green on the first run with the new leg (m2): a probe agent raised the card, the box shell went `VERIFY_INLINE_TOKEN` unset to set, and the store's sha256 matched the submitted value (`4f9028d7bb7aa2c8…`, never printed). `verify-dashboard --offline` 49/49 (8 new), `verify-gateway-reads` and `verify-windows` green, roster 4 before and 4 after. The receipt that survives the session is the ledger entry in docs/PLUMBING-AUDIT.md §3 dated 2026-09-06, which carries the store digest and the gate tallies; the run logs themselves are scratch files under the session's scratchpad (`env-connector-plane.log` and its siblings for the ENV-1 re-run) and do not outlive it. The leg is INCONCLUSIVE rather than red when the model does not raise the card in time, and takes GATE-8's exit 3 so a green run means one thing | M |
| ENV-1 · **landed** (2026-09-06, bundle 86cce95a) | `setShellSecret {field: VERIFY_ENV_3}` answered `{stored: true, applied: true}` and `probeShellSecret` agreed, while the agent "Chief of staff" -- which has its own desktop window on display :4 -- ran `printenv VERIFY_ENV_3 \| wc -c; env \| grep -c '^VERIFY_ENV_3='` through its shell tool and got **0 and 0** (measured on the Mac box 2026-09-06 13:45). The box does not have one shell: `loopback-sand-box.ts` `ensureWindow` starts a per-window exec daemon behind the 1339 router, the window router sends every command from an agent with a window to it, and `box-exec-daemon/server.ts` gives each daemon its own `#environment`. `applyEnvironment` pushed to `primaryEndpoint()` only, and so did `applyStoredShellSecrets` at bring-up -- so the credential landed in the one shell no windowed agent ever uses | `applyEnvironment` fans out: the primary endpoint AND every live window in `windowConnections`, each pinged first, with a window that will not take the update named in the answer rather than swallowed. `ensureWindow` applies the CURRENT stored shell-secret environment to a new window's daemon before the window is handed back, so a desktop opened after a key was stored never runs a command without it. The `{applied, pendingWindows}` pair rides out through `pushShellEnvSecretsToBox`, `setShellSecret`, `deleteShellSecret`, the inline card's sink and the ack the model reads, so `applied: true` means EVERY shell took it. Measured: `tests/env-fanout.test.mjs` 9 cases, 683 unit tests green, both typechecks clean; on the Mac box with bundle 86cce95a built, deployed and in-box identical, `verify-connector-plane --shell-secrets` green on the first run with the new legs -- a probe agent holding display :2 read `CODERABBIT_API_KEY` unset -> set -> unset in its OWN shell alongside the primary, and the same through the model-raised card for `VERIFY_INLINE_TOKEN` (store sha256 `329a982adea6e933...`, never printed). `verify-windows` 14/14 with nothing left on the box, `verify-gateway-reads` OK, `verify-dashboard --offline` 50/50, roster 4 before and 4 after. **Skeptic pass, same day:** a pending window is named by the AGENT holding the seat (`<agent id> (display :4)`) rather than by the shared box id the map key carries, and `SharedDesktopSandBox` does that renaming because it is the only layer that holds index -> agent; the window seed carries the operator's BOX secrets as well as the shell store, and the box-secrets `replace: true` push carries the shell store so saving one credential plane no longer wipes the other (what replace still drops -- PATH, HOME, DISPLAY -- the `/bin/sh -lc` login shell restores from /etc/profile); and (m) asks its windowed question a second time with the store emptied on disk, because a windowed probe re-pushes the whole store on its way to the accessor and so cannot otherwise tell the write's fan-out from its own bring-up push | M |

| TENANT-1 · **landed** (2026-09-07; built on two branches, merged in 689abf4 and 792fa89, reconciled in d3ee947, then measured against the live Coolify and fixed in the commit that carries this row) | Every customer is supposed to get their own instance and their own sandbox, and nothing existed to make one: a tenant was a hand install on the R750, a hand-minted gateway token and one shared relay password read once at boot, with no account list and no way for a person to sign in as themselves. What shipped is `cp/`, one Node 22 service with no npm dependency at all (`node:sqlite` store, `node:crypto` scrypt and HMAC, `node:http` api). Accounts with a per-account scrypt hash (N 2^15, r 8, p 1, 64 byte key, `maxmem` raised because 2^15 needs 33.5 MB and node's default ceiling is 32) that no route returns; a 12 h session `v1.<claims>.<HMAC-SHA256 of that text>` minted here and verified by the same `cp/session.mjs` the relay wave imports; a sign-in that answers the same 401 in the same time for a wrong address and a wrong password and locks after ten failures per email and per visitor in ten minutes. Provisioning is seven idempotent steps written to a ledger (directories, secrets, compose, service, envs, urls, start), with `--dry-run` doing every read and every render and creating nothing, and a retry that starts at the step that failed rather than building a second instance beside the first or minting a second gateway token. Each tenant gets its own gateway token and relay password; neither is ever in the compose text and the relay password is shown once. Also `cp/cli.mjs` (the password is prompted, never an argument), `cp/Dockerfile` (node:22-alpine pinned by digest, running as an unprivileged uid so the files it writes are readable over ssh without sudo; it said 1000 here and TENANT-2 measured `id -u sem` on the R750 at 1001 and made the uid a build argument), `deploy/coolify/control-plane.compose.yml` (one bind of `/data/titanbot`, no ports, no docker socket), `cp/README.md`, `docs/TENANCY.md` and the gate `scripts/verify-control-plane.mjs`. **Measured on this Mac (Darwin 25.5.0, node 22.23.1):** suite 832 pass 0 fail; gate 101 PASS 0 FAIL against the real `cp/server.mjs`, not a stub. **Measured from this Mac against the R750's live Coolify 4.0.0 (project Titanium Computing `c24e2ulqhmgn4d0c5lx43i63`, server `zl2ti5llrtpx83918j8arb9f`), read only, 2026-09-07 04:58:** `tenant adopt titanium p927bfqm83ioloibamlvyd7g console.titanium.bot` wrote the ledger row and changed nothing on the service; `GET /v1/tenants/titanium` came back `status adopted`, `coolify.reachable true`, `coolify.status running`, containers `titanbot-relay` (`running:unknown`, fqdn `tb.semfreak.dev:7777,console.titanium.bot:7777`) and `titanbot-box` (`running:unknown`); `tenant add demo Demo --dry-run` printed the seven steps ending POST /services, POST /services/{uuid}/envs, PATCH /services/{uuid}, POST /services/{uuid}/start with a compose sha256 and no values; and afterwards `GET /services` still listed 6 services with only `titanbot` among them (no `titanbot-demo`), and the tenant root held 0 entries. **One defect that dry run found and this commit fixes:** `GET /services/{uuid}/applications`, the route the openapi documents as the only place a container status lives, answers `404 {"message":"Not found."}` on this Coolify 4.0.0, the same way the per-component PATCH did in DOMAIN-1, so a running tenant read as `unknown` with no containers. That build returns a service object richer than its own documented schema instead (service-level `status`, `server_status`, an inline `applications` array of `{uuid, name, fqdn, status}`), so `readCoolifyState` reads the object first and asks the sub-route only when the object carries nothing; three tests pin both shapes | Nothing is deployed yet: the remaining operator steps are on the R750 and were done by hand in this row's plan. TENANT-2 turned them into `deploy/r750/control-plane-install.sh` and `deploy/r750/control-plane-coolify.mjs`, and `docs/TENANCY.md` section 9 is now those two rather than a dozen clicks. What it cost was exactly that: a second container, a second hostname, a second certificate, a second copy of the console's environment, a second `auth.json` with a password somebody had to be handed, and a second thing to redeploy on every ship, per customer. TENANT-5 keeps everything underneath it (the token, the derivation, the store, the scrypt parameters, the lockout, the account routes, the shared release directory, the endpoint guard, the plain-word copy) and replaces the arrangement above it: one relay, one console, one login page, a tenant resolved per request from the session. The row below is the original, kept because its measurements are still the measurements. The relay side of the same thing. A tenant relay has one door, its own password read once at boot from `ui/auth.json`, so a control plane session opens nothing; and its writable files (`auth.json`, `subscriptions.json`, the job bus token, mail) sit in the `ui` directory that every tenant shares read-write, so two tenants would write over each other's settings and the shared mount can never be made read-only **Closed out 2026-09-07 03:00 to 03:40 CDT (this Mac + the R750):** the operator steps the fix stage owed were run: Jason's instance moved its auth, endpoints and subscriptions into /home/sem/titanbot/state (move-relay-state.sh copy, the compose pasted, a quiet restart, deploy gate 56/56, then clean; ui/ is code now), CP_RELAY_PEERS=66.90.191.45/32 set on the control plane and it restarted, and demo was stopped, deleted and provisioned again on the fixed render (Coolify service sy74dau8ilh1g4u7a9eaw8f8): the first try failed at the hostname with Coolify's 409 domain conflict from the just-deleted service, the retry from the failed step ran to running in 80 s, and the provisioner now takes that one refusal with force_domain_override (2aec871, two tests). Live proofs after: verify-tenant-browser 14/14 (demo signs in at demo.titanium.bot and sees its first agent; demo at console.titanium.bot is sent to its own instance signed in; a wrong password reads in plain words), verify-tenant live 13 PASS 0 FAIL 13 SKIP (the skips need credentials the gate was not given), verify-deploy 56/56. Open: TENANT-3, TENANT-4, the Coolify key rotation, and Jason's own account. | `SAND_UI_STATE_DIR` so those writable files come out of the tenant's own `state/` directory, then the relay accepting a session in place of the password: `verifySessionToken` imported from `cp/session.mjs` (the same file, not a second implementation) against the `CP_SESSION_SECRET` it is given, and the token's `tenant` claim checked against its own `TENANT_ID` so a validly signed session for another customer is refused. Jason's adopted instance keeps its password as well, because he also signs in from the tailnet. **Shipped in this wave:** one session-token module (`ui/session-token.mjs`, node builtins only, `cp/session.mjs` re-exports it so there is one implementation and not two); `SAND_UI_STATE_DIR` as the single setting that moves `auth.json`, `endpoints.json`, `subscriptions.json`, `mail.json` and `mail-inbox.jsonl` into the tenant's own directory, with each file's own older setting still winning and each written file taking its directory's owner; account sign-in on the relay behind the three-variable tenant mode (`TENANT_ID`, `CP_URL`, `CP_SESSION_SECRET`), including the 302 to another tenant's host with `?sso=` when a customer types the wrong address, `GET /login?sso=` at the other end, plain-word answers for a wrong password and for a control plane that is not answering, and the lockout counting email attempts the way it counts password attempts; a tenant relay that comes up with no docker socket at all, one `dockerAvailable()` probe deciding, and a named refusal rather than a hang or an empty 200 on each surface that used to shell out; the gate `scripts/verify-tenant.mjs` with a fake control plane it starts itself; and the deploy tooling `deploy/r750/control-plane-install.sh` plus `deploy/r750/control-plane-coolify.mjs`, both idempotent, both with a dry run, which replace the hand steps TENANT-1's row ends on. `cp/Dockerfile` takes the uid as a build argument defaulting to 1001, which is what `id -u sem` answers on the R750; it was 1001 and the file said 1000, so every tenant directory would have come out owned by a uid with no account behind it. **Measured 2026-09-07, landed.** On this Mac (Darwin 25.5.0, node 22.23.1): `npm test` 894 pass 0 fail in 30.7 s, up from 854 at the start of the wave; `verify-dashboard --offline` 58 PASS 0 FAIL; `verify-tenant` with the fake control plane and two relays it starts itself, 59 PASS 0 FAIL 0 SKIP. From this Mac in headless Chrome, 14 checks 0 failing: the demo account signs in at its own address and gets a roster drawn from its own box, the same account typed into `console.titanium.bot` is redirected to `demo.titanium.bot` signed in, and a wrong password says `That email or password is not right.` with no session and no status code on the page. Four things the live run found that no test had: Coolify owns the name `COOLIFY_URL` and overwrites it in every service container (the setting is `CP_COOLIFY_URL` now); a compose service never sees an environment value its own file does not name, so the base compose names the three tenancy variables; Coolify creates an environment field for every `${VAR}` when it creates a service, so setting one is a POST that falls back to a PATCH on the 409; and a running service takes a restart, not a start | M |
| TENANT-3 · **shipped and enforcing; two live defects found and fixed** (2026-09-09) | **The exposure is nine ports on four addresses, not four on one.** Re-measured from inside the demo tenant's box on the R750 2026-09-08, with `bash` `/dev/tcp` and a 1.1.1.1:443 sanity leg first: OPEN on **22, 47291, 8000, 80, 443, 2049, 445, 11434 and 5000** against each of the box's default gateway `192.168.32.1`, its `titanbot-net` gateway `192.168.48.1`, the tailnet address `100.110.83.82` and docker0 `172.17.0.1`. `ss -lntp` names them: sshd on 22 **and 47291**, docker-proxy on 8000 (Coolify), smbd on 445, ollama on 11434, a python service on 5000, NFS on 2049. So it is not two login prompts and a hosting panel; it is those plus the machine's file exports and its local model server. The box has no IPv6 default route today, so v6 is a future path and not a current one, but sshd and Coolify both listen on `[::]`. **The first probe of this was a false negative and is discarded:** it used `sh`, which is dash on the box image and has no `/dev/tcp`, so it read every port as shut. **And the remedy this row used to prescribe could not work.** It said `-i br-<id> -d <gateway> -j DROP` in INPUT. That is right for 22 and 47291 and wrong for 8000, the port the row is about: `iptables -t nat -S` carries `-A PREROUTING -m addrtype --dst-type LOCAL -j DOCKER` then `-A DOCKER ! -i br-7ef42af3f026 -p tcp --dport 8000 -j DNAT --to-destination 10.0.2.5:8080`, and `br-7ef42af3f026` is Coolify's own bridge, so a packet from a box bridge is DNATed to Coolify's container before INPUT is consulted and then forwarded, never delivered locally. An INPUT rule would have dropped two ports and silently done nothing for the third. **The gate that was supposed to prove this proved nothing about it:** `--verify` scanned box to box only, so the verify-deploy isolation leg was green while every host port stood open to every tenant | **Built 2026-09-08.** `deploy/r750/box-isolation.sh` installs a second table, `inet titanbot_host`: chain `host` at `type filter hook prerouting priority -250` (before docker's nat prerouting at -100, so the destination is still the host's own address), matching `fib daddr type local`, SYN only, `iifname "br-*"` and `docker0` (the bridge family, present or future, rather than one bridge name), jumping to a chain that exempts and then counts or drops per port. Drop set `{22, 47291, 8000}`; watch-only `{2049, 445, 11434, 5000, 80, 443}`, which join the drop set once their counters read zero. **The exemptions fail closed:** Coolify drives this host over SSH from inside its own container (measured: four live sessions `10.0.2.5` -> `10.0.0.1:22`), so it arrives on a docker bridge exactly like a customer's box and `iifname "br-*"` covers it too; the coolify container and the control plane are resolved to addresses at apply time, and if one is present but unreadable the script installs **nothing** and exits non-zero, because the timer would otherwise re-apply a bad drop set every 60 s. **It shadows before it drops:** `TITANBOT_HOST_GUARD` defaults to `shadow`, the same matches with counters and no verdict, `--counters` reads them per port, and the mode file `/etc/titanbot/host-guard.mode` is what the timer reads. `--verify` gained the box-to-host leg (bash `/dev/tcp`, every host address the box can name, drop-set ports fail the run and watch-only ports are reported), and `scripts/verify-deploy.mjs` carries it as a leg of its own. Remaining operator action: read `--counters` after the shadow window and set the mode to `drop`. **Two things the shadow pass could not have told anybody, both found on the R750 2026-09-09 with the guard ALREADY in drop mode, both fixed.** (1) *The exemption was IPv4 only and the drop is not.* It was emitted as one `ip saddr` line, which in an `inet` table matches IPv4 alone, while the two drop lines match on `iifname` and so carry both families. Measured from inside the coolify container, before any change: `10.0.0.1:22` answered with the real SSH banner and `[fdb8:a9ef:e4a4::1]:22` answered nothing at all, with port 8000 over v6 reading `http 000`. The coolify bridge `br-7ef42af3f026` carries a global v6 prefix, its gateway address on that bridge is `fib daddr type local`, sshd listens on `[::]:22` and `[::]:47291` with docker-proxy on `[::]:8000`, and five coolify containers hold global v6 addresses there. Coolify is the one container that must never be cut off, because it is how this machine is administered; nothing had broken only because Coolify addresses the host by its v4 address today, and one AAAA answer would have changed that. A counter says a packet matched, never which family it would have been refused on, so no length of shadow window would have surfaced it. The exemption now emits one line per family a container really holds; the drop stays family-agnostic on purpose, because a v6 carve-out there would be a hole in the boundary itself. **AFTER, same probe, same minute:** `[fdb8:a9ef:e4a4::1]:22` returns `SSH-2.0-OpenSSH_9.6p1`, v6 port 8000 returns `http 401`, and the box boundary is unmoved — the demo box still reads 22, 47291 and 8000 closed and 80, 443 and the 1.1.1.1 sanity leg open. `box-isolation.sh --verify` PASS across all three tenant boxes; a new connection from the control plane to the Coolify API answers `HTTP/1.1 401`; console.titanium.bot 401, api.titanium.bot 404, titanium.bot 200, unchanged either side. (2) *The counters were never accumulating, so the drop set's evidence did not exist.* The fingerprint that lets a tick keep the table it already installed is written to `/run/titanbot`, which is root-owned 0755, while the unit is a `systemd --user` service running as the operator and asking for root only for `nft` — and both the `mkdir` and the write were swallowed by `|| true`. Measured: the fingerprint file did not exist at all, and the accept rule's handle walked 2086 to 2088 to 2090 across 90 seconds with its counter resetting each time, so every counter ever read on this host showed at most the last minute. The boundary held the whole while; the instrument did not. The state now defaults under `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (user-writable, cleared on reboot, the same property `/run` was chosen for) and falls back to `/run` for a root run, and it says so when it still cannot write. **AFTER:** handle 4 stable across three reads spanning 140 s and two timer ticks, and the service logs `the counters were left running`. **Consequently 2049, 445 and 11434 go back to watch-only.** Not a change of mind about NFS, Samba and ollama: the 31-minute accumulating window quoted for them could not have existed on this host, so the rule this row set for itself was not actually met. That expansion had never reached the R750 either way — the live table still read them watch-only — so nothing was taken away; what was removed is a default that would have applied a restrictive change to a host where about sixty containers belong to other products and 11434 is the machine's own model server, on evidence that was not what it said it was. **Owner: Jason. Next action:** read `--counters` over a window the counters are now really accumulating over, and move the three back into the drop set if they still read zero **Three more, 2026-09-09.** (3) *A customer's box reaches the host's NFS and SMB today, and that was being carried as watch-only.* Probed from inside the demo box with `bash` `/dev/tcp` and a 1.1.1.1:443 sanity leg that answered OPEN: 22, 47291 and 8000 are shut on all seven host addresses tested (bridge gateways 192.168.32.1 and 192.168.48.1, docker0 172.17.0.1, tailnet 100.110.83.82, LAN 192.168.0.9/.12/.98) -- the guard holds -- while **80, 443, 2049, 445, 11434 and 5000 answer OPEN on all seven**. With the fingerprint fix landed the counters finally accumulate, and over the current window 2049, 445, 11434 and 5000 each read 30 packets: **non-zero**, which independently confirms the "zero over 31 minutes" that justified the reverted expansion could not have been true, and also shows other containers on this shared host really do use these ports. So a blanket drop stays wrong and the decision splits: **2049 and 445 now have a BOX-SOURCE-SCOPED drop** (`TITANBOT_HOST_GUARD_BOX_DROP_PORTS`, default `2049,445`, matched on the box addresses the script already enumerates and applied inside the same `guarded` chain), which takes the host's file exports away from a customer's sandbox and costs the other ~60 containers nothing. 11434 (the machine's own model server) and 5000 (an unidentified python service) stay watch-only until 5000 is identified. (4) *The verify leg could not see the regression it exists to catch.* It probed only host addresses derivable from inside a box -- the default gateway and each attached subnet's `.1` -- so every address it named was a bridge gateway; the tailnet and LAN doors had to be probed by hand. `TITANBOT_HOST_ADDRS` existed as the escape hatch and nothing set it. The leg now also discovers the host's own addresses on the host side, where the script already runs as root (`ip -o -4 addr show` minus bridge, veth, docker0 and lo), and probes them in the same loop, so the gate is evidence for every door rather than for the bridge gateways only. (5) *An exemption was claimable from the segment it exempts.* The `guarded` chain's first rule accepted on `ip saddr`/`ip6 saddr` alone with no input interface, and `coolify-proxy` holds 192.168.32.3 on the demo box's own service network (the box is .2 there) and 192.168.48.2 on titanbot-net (the box is .6). `docker inspect` reports `CapAdd=[] CapDrop=[] Privileged=false` for that box, i.e. docker's default set including NET_RAW, so a tenant with root inside its own box could forge an exempt source on a segment it already shares and be accepted for the whole drop set. Not attempted -- that would be an attack on a live host with a customer on it. Each exemption is now emitted as `iifname "<the bridge that address lives on>" ip saddr <addr> accept`, per network, derived the same way `BR` already is, and a bridge name that is not an interface on this host trips the fail-closed latch. The control plane keeps its way in: its default route is 192.168.16.1, not titanbot-net, so scoping costs it nothing **APPLIED AND MEASURED ON THE R750 2026-09-09, live, with the operator online.** BEFORE, from inside the demo box against all seven host addresses: 22, 47291 and 8000 refused everywhere; **2049, 445, 11434, 5000, 80 and 443 open on all seven**; the 1.1.1.1:443 sanity leg open. AFTER, same probe, same box: **2049 and 445 refused on all seven**, 11434, 5000, 80 and 443 still open (watch-only), 22/47291/8000 still refused, sanity leg still open. The way back was the previous script, which the 60 s timer re-runs, and it was not needed. Checked in the same minutes: a NEW connection from inside the coolify container to `10.0.0.1:22` opens; `coolify-sentinel` still pushes to `host.docker.internal:8000` and its health reads 200; Coolify's own API on the host reads 200; console.titanium.bot 401, api.titanium.bot 404, titanium.bot 200, all unchanged; all three boxes running with unchanged `StartedAt`. `box-isolation.sh --verify` PASS, now over **18 box-to-host probes across three boxes** instead of 6, the extra twelve being the tailnet and LAN addresses the leg could not previously see. **One correction to the counter evidence:** the watch-only counters read 50 packets each and the drop-set counters 104 each at the moment they were read, which is the shape of a probe sweep and its SYN retransmits rather than of other containers' traffic -- so "the counters are non-zero, therefore other containers use these ports" is not established either way. It does not change the decision: the box-scoped drop is correct whoever else uses those ports, which is the whole reason for scoping it. | M |

| TENANT-5 · **LANDED, measured on the R750 2026-09-07 04:59 to 06:00 CDT** | TENANT-2 gave every customer their own relay on their own hostname, and Jason refused the shape the day it shipped: "In my mind there should only be one extra Docker container per tenant. Every time we add somebody new, we're basically duplicating everything. That sounds crazy." Four things follow from taking that literally. (a) One relay, one console, one login page at `console.titanium.bot`; `<slug>.titanium.bot` is retired and the wildcard record is now decoration. (b) A tenant is one box container plus `/data/titanbot/<slug>/`, and the per-tenant `state/auth.json` is deleted rather than left on disk as a credential that opens nothing. (c) The relay resolves ONE tenant per request from the session cookie and threads it through every seam: `/api`, the event stream, the VNC bridge, endpoints, the job bus, mail, the runtime bundle. (d) The relay holds the docker socket for the whole fleet, which is what makes the model picker, the connectors editor and the desktop buttons work for every customer instead of being honestly absent on each. **The one measured surprise, and it is load bearing:** `coolify-proxy` on the R750 is `traefik:v3.6` started with `--providers.docker=true` and NO `--providers.docker.network`, and the relay container carries no `traefik.docker.network` label. Traefik's fallback for a multi-homed container is the first entry of a Go map, and Go randomises map order per iteration, so putting the relay on a second network without pinning that label makes `console.titanium.bot` answer 502 at random after any provider refresh: not reproducible, not attributable to the deploy that caused it. The pin is in the compose and `scripts/verify-deploy.mjs` reads it back off the running container after every restart, because a stripped label has to be caught by a gate rather than by the console going down days later. **Also measured on the live host:** every Coolify service sits on exactly one bridge named after its own service uuid, each container carries two aliases, `coolify-proxy` is attached to all 13 service networks, both live boxes are named exactly `titanbot-box-<service uuid>` (which is what lets the registry compute a box name with no schema change), and Coolify's parser (`bootstrap/helpers/shared.php`) keeps a service's declared networks, merges its own uuid network in beside them, and leaves a top-level network entry alone when the compose declares it, so `external: true` is accepted as written. **What this commit carries:** `scripts/verify-one-console.mjs` (four suites: the registry route's four auth cases including that the master's bytes are nowhere in the body, two customers on one console getting two rosters off two boxes with two tokens plus the cross-check, the unknown-workspace answer, and the operator's door with a copy that has no control plane at all); `deploy/r750/one-console-migrate.sh` (idempotent, prints rather than clicks, `TITANBOT_DRY_RUN=1` changes nothing, and it keeps the network attach and the code ship in two separate restarts with a check between so a 502 can be attributed); the relay and control-plane compose changes (`titanbot-net`, the traefik pin, `CP_RELAY_TOKEN`, `SAND_BOX_CONTAINER` required, `TENANT_ID` and `CP_SESSION_SECRET` gone); one new leg in `verify-deploy`; and `docs/TENANCY.md` rewritten to this shape with the migration table left as *to be measured*. **Two deviations from the contract, both deliberate and both in the docs.** The relay's own registry entry is seeded from its environment and NOT from the control plane's adoption row, because that row holds a uuid and a host and carries no token and no directories, and because a control plane outage must not take Jason's console with it. And the mail webhook routes by DOMAIN rather than by a shared domain with a unique agent name: agent names are not unique across customers, so the moment two of them each have a Titan, `titan@titanium.bot` is ambiguous. **Measured on this Mac (Darwin 25.5.0, node 22.23.1, docker compose v5.1.4), 2026-09-07:** `npm test` 918 pass 0 fail; both compose files validate with stock `docker compose config`; the migration script's dry run reads this Mac and reports every step as still to do; `verify-one-console` exits 2 with a named reason until `ui/tenant-registry.mjs` lands, and with a stub in place its own machinery measures 26 legs green and 8 red, each red one a TENANT-5 behaviour the relay wave still owes **CLOSED OUT ON THE LIVE SERVER 2026-09-07, 04:59 to 06:00 CDT, from this Mac (Darwin 25.5.0, node 22.23.1) against the R750 (docker 29.0.0, Coolify 4.0.0).** The three waves were merged onto `webdevtodayjason/gb`, the pieces made to agree, and the migration run in the order section 22 fixes. **On this Mac:** `npm test` **971 pass 0 fail** in 31 s (904 at the start of the wave); `verify-dashboard --offline` **58 PASS 0 FAIL**; `verify-one-console` local **48 PASS 0 FAIL 0 SKIP**; `verify-control-plane` **115 PASS 0 FAIL**. **On the R750:** `titanbot-net` created, subnet `192.168.48.0/20`; Jason's relay attached and the console back to 200 within **50 seconds** of each restart; relay networks `p927bfqm83ioloibamlvyd7g titanbot-net` with pin `titanbot-net` and `coolify-proxy` on that network; the control plane rebuilt, on both networks, health `{"ok":true,"version":"1.0.0","tenants":2,"accounts":1}`; `GET /v1/relay/tenants` **401** with no bearer, **401** with the admin token, **200** with the relay credential; the relay boot line `work 2: demo, titanium`. Demo stopped, deleted (data kept, 41 MB at `/data/titanbot/demo`) and provisioned again as ONE box in **23 seconds**. A second customer signed up end to end through `POST /v1/signups` in **16 seconds**: account, slug derived from the company name, one box. **The live proofs:** headless Chrome, two browser contexts at `console.titanium.bot`, **17 PASS 0 FAIL**, with no agent id on both rosters (`c63fdce4-4fc0-4ea7-8a1b-93657df2c6c5` and `d7df78a5-3c3d-471d-9d13-ef3d9535f9e8`); `verify-one-console` live **18 PASS 0 FAIL 8 SKIP**; `verify-deploy` **58 PASS 0 FAIL 2 inconclusive**; `verify-mail` read-only **16 PASS 0 FAIL**. Over HTTP, three rosters from one console and no overlap: demo `New Bot c63fdce4…`, north-bay `New Bot d7df78a5…`, the operator `Titan 96a720b6…, Scribe f97bfb2e…`. **The server is now four containers:** one relay, one control plane, and one box each for Jason and demo. Adding a customer adds one. **FOUR THINGS THE LIVE RUN FOUND THAT NO TEST COULD.** (1) The Traefik pin cannot be a variable: Coolify rewrites every dollar-brace inside a `labels:` block to an escaped one, so the label reached the container as the literal text `${TITANBOT_PROXY_NETWORK}` even though the value was set and was in the generated `.env`. The pin is a literal `titanbot-net` now, and that is worse-than-nothing failure caught only because the gate reads the label back off the running container. (2) The same label-first bug TENANT-5 deletes from the relay was in the migration script and in `verify-deploy`: `docker ps --filter label=com.titanbot.role=relay` taking the first name answered DEMO's relay, so the script printed demo's network as the pin (which would have taken the console down) and the deploy gate had been measuring a customer's containers. Both now match on the role label AND the Coolify service uuid. (3) The relay logged `titanium is not on this console` every 60 seconds on a console that was working perfectly, because the control plane correctly leaves the operator's row out; the operator's own skip is silent now. (4) The control plane had to join `titanbot-net`: it waits for a new box to answer on `http://titanbot-box-<uuid>:1340`, an address only that network carries, so from its own network the wait always fell through to Coolify's container status, which says started and not answering. | **Landed. Nothing is owed on this row.** The migration ran in the order the row demanded, network before code, with a check between: (a) `titanbot-net` created, no restart; (b) the compose pasted and Jason's service restarted in a quiet window of its own, console confirmed at 200 before anything else moved; (c) the relay and control plane source shipped and the control plane image rebuilt; (d) `CP_RELAY_TOKEN` generated on the server into `cp.env` and set on both resources, the registry route confirmed to answer the relay and refuse the admin token; (e) demo re-provisioned as one box and its two-container service retired, data directory kept; (f) the browser proof, then the gates. Step (b) took two restarts rather than one, and the reason is the Traefik-pin finding above; the rollback was never needed because the console answered 200 after both. `scripts/verify-tenant.mjs` and `scripts/verify-tenant-browser.mjs` are deleted rather than left red: they measured the TENANT-2 shape (one relay per customer, per-tenant hostnames, the wrong-address redirect), and every leg of theirs that is still true moved to `scripts/verify-one-console.mjs`, `scripts/verify-one-console-browser.mjs`, `tests/relay-docker-absent.test.mjs`, `tests/relay-one-console.test.mjs` and `tests/relay-tenant-endpoints.test.mjs`. `tests/cp-relay-pair.test.mjs` is new and closes the one hole the three waves left: it starts the real control plane and a real relay and points the second at the first, so a field one side renames and the other still reads is caught by `npm test` rather than by a customer. **Still open, and neither is TENANT-5's:** TENANT-3 (the host's own 22 and 8000 from a box, now across two bridges per box) and TENANT-4. **Two smaller ones, owned:** the instance-password legs of `verify-one-console` were not measured, because Jason's console password is not written anywhere a script should read; the operator's door was measured over the gateway bearer instead, which resolves to the same workspace. And a brand new workspace can answer "not available" for up to 60 seconds, which is the relay's registry refresh interval and closes itself. | L |

And 15:12: "we should capture tries, users and passwords tried and report in the Super admin for failures with IP." Before this there was no super admin at all: every account on the control plane was one kind of account, the operator's only tool was `cp/cli.mjs` over ssh, and a refused sign-in left one log line on the relay that nothing read and nothing kept. Nobody could answer "is somebody attacking the console right now" without tailing a container's stdout, and nothing anywhere could tell one address trying the same wrong password forty times from one address trying forty different ones | **Landed 2026-09-07** (8d97d4d, 6ea8a4e). A super admin flag and a disabled flag on accounts, both defaulting to off so an upgrade of the R750's database gives nobody the console and locks nobody out; `account promote` / `account demote` with the last one refused. A failed sign-in ledger at both doors: `ui/login-ledger.mjs` writes `<state dir>/login-attempts.jsonl` rotating at 5 MB keeping one previous file, and the control plane keeps its own `login_attempts` table so an attempt that skipped the console is still visible. **The password decision:** the tried password is kept only as HMAC-SHA256 under a 32 byte salt made once at 0600 per service, never the text, because that is the least that can tell a stale saved password from an attack; a successful sign-in gets no hash at all. Six or more distinct passwords from one address inside a sliding ten minute window raises an Attack chip. Five read routes and six actions under `/v1/admin`, behind CP_ADMIN_TOKEN or a session whose account carries the flag, **looked up in the store on every request** so a demotion takes effect on the next call rather than in up to twelve hours. `cp/admin/` serves the page: three files, no framework, no CDN, its own CSP, five panels (sign-in attempts, clients and users, box health, system health, payments saying it is not connected yet).
