No Coolify recreate, BOX-6). **Measured live from this Mac against the running system, 2026-09-07 17:52 to 17:55 CDT:** `scripts/verify-deploy.mjs --url https://console.titanium.bot` 59 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 inconclusive, exit 0. `GET /admin` 200, 5805 bytes, its CSS and JS 200. Every one of the five read routes answers 401 with no session and 401 to a near-miss token. `GET /v1/admin/system`: host load 8.32/5.67/4.53, memory 2.16 TB total with 2.05 TB available, `/data/titanbot` 2.83 TB with 2.06 TB free, Coolify reachable, relay reachable; the archives mount, the nightly backup, the isolation verdict and the mail webhook each answer `measured:false` with the sentence that says what would fix it, rather than a zero. `GET /v1/admin/boxes`: titanium, demo and richard-avery, all three containers running, gateway answering in 6, 15 and 4 ms, 897204 / 112404 / 546576 KB of disk and 6.87 / 1.28 / 2.86 GB of memory. `GET /v1/admin/clients`: three workspaces, two users, plan `none` on each, no password hash anywhere in the answer. **The ledger and the attack rule, on real traffic:** the deploy gate's own refused sign-ins landed in the relay's file and the control plane read them over `GET /admin/login-attempts`; three further distinct passwords from the same address took it to 14 rows and 6 distinct passwords in the ten minute window, and the summary flipped to `attack: true` with `passwordStory: "6 different passwords"`. Every digest is 64 hex characters or empty, and no answer carried a password. **Open until:** Jason runs the two commands in docs/ADMIN.md (`account add jason@... titanium`, then `account promote jason@...`) -- `superAdmins` reads 0 today, so the console currently refuses every account and only CP_ADMIN_TOKEN opens it -- and the console is opened once against real customers. Those 11 are the retroactive clause's rows (agent "node" or empty, instance door, refused or locked, before GATE_LABEL_BEFORE). What does NOT work: `node scripts/verify-deploy.mjs --url https://console.titanium.bot` was run at **19:38-19:39 UTC after the ship, 62 PASS, 0 FAIL, 2 inconclusive**, and its 11 login and lockout rows arrived in the relay's ledger carrying the **exact agent `titanbot-gate/verify-deploy`** (read back through GET /v1/admin/sign-ins from inside titanbot-cp), so item C's half landed and the relay records it with **no relay change and no relay restart**. But every one of those 11 rows reads `gate: false`, because the second half of the rule needs a SUCCESSFUL operator or super-admin sign-in from the same address inside the hour, and **verify-deploy deliberately holds no password** ("This gate does not know the console password and no longer asks for one", scripts/verify-deploy.mjs header): it is let in by the gateway bearer, which is a page request and writes no ledger row. So on a normal deploy-gate run the Attack pill stays, and it did: 147.136.44.142 still reads Attack at 24 hours (116 tries, 24 distinct passwords) and at 72 hours (209 tries, 53 distinct). The rule is right and unclaimable; the gate that makes the noise simply cannot satisfy it. Filed as **SIGNIN-1c**, not left as a note. **REOPENED AND CLOSED AGAIN 2026-09-09 22:00 to 22:45 UTC by a review of this row's own shipped code, and the rule changed rather than the wiring.** A review measured the second half of the label on this Mac and it does not hold: fixture of one successful operator instance-door sign-in from 203.0.113.9 at 12:00Z and eight refusals with eight distinct passwords from the same address at 12:01 to 12:08Z, all after GATE_LABEL_BEFORE. With agent `curl/8` the address read **attack true, attempts 9, distinctInWindow 8**. With `titanbot-gate/verify-deploy` on the identical rows it read **attack FALSE, attempts 1, distinctInWindow 0**, and summariseByPassword skipped them too. The half called "the part an outsider cannot fake" is a NEIGHBOURING SIGN-IN FROM THE SAME ADDRESS, which is an office NAT, a VPN egress or a borrowed laptop away from any stranger, so the pair was a switch anyone sharing an egress address with the operator could throw. **So the label stops deciding anything.** markGateRows still marks, and summariseByAddress, summariseByAccount and summariseByPassword now COUNT every marked row: gateRows is an annotation beside the number, never a subtraction. With nothing being subtracted the header needs no second half, and losing that second half is what closed the gate half of this row: the deploy gate holds no password, so no successful operator sign-in ever sits beside its rows. The dated clause keeps the address test, because its only evidence is the row's shape, and the two clauses now answer separately (`gates.named`, `gates.older`) and print different sentences. A row that GOT IN is a sign-in on both clauses now, which the row always said and only the legacy branch did. **MEASURED ON THE R750 2026-09-09 22:37 to 22:45 UTC** (cp rebuilt from a6d2cf1 and restarted through Coolify at 22:37:31; from this Mac, Darwin 25.6.0, node v22.23.1, headless Chromium through playwright-core 1.62.1 at 1440x900, operator token seeded into sessionStorage, Jason's account untouched). GET /v1/admin/sign-ins from inside titanbot-cp: **11 of 11 rows carrying `titanbot-gate/verify-deploy` read gate true, gateScript verify-deploy, gateWhy named** where every one of them read gate false before; gates answers **{rows 22, named 11, older 11}**. On screen: 22 grey rows, **11 reading "says it is our own verification gate (verify-deploy)"** and **11 reading "an older row, from before gates named themselves"**; the strip reads **LOOK LIKE OUR OWN GATES 22, counted like everything else, marked in grey below**; and **ATTACKS still reads 1** on 147.136.44.142, whose row now carries "22 of them look like our own gates, and are counted above" beside **131 attempts, 39 refused, 65 locked out, 27 ok, 28 different passwords**. Unit: cp-signins-gate 9/9 rewritten around the new contract, including a case that fails if a header ever moves a number again; verify-admin **503 PASS 0 FAIL** on this Mac. **And the 4-of-46 split is now checked rather than commented.** The same review asked whether the untouched gates are quietly writing the rows the pill counts. Grepped on this Mac 2026-09-09: 46 files match scripts/verify-*.mjs, 4 import scripts/gate-agent.mjs, and exactly 7 name a login path at all -- the three that post one at a live door plus verify-admin (its own control plane and relay on a throwaway directory), verify-browser-tools (its own fixture login page on 127.0.0.1), verify-console-polish (reads with a bearer) and verify-job-bus (checks that an unauthenticated call is bounced to /login). The candidates the review suggested -- verify-mail, verify-dashboard, verify-marketplace, verify-handoff, verify-connector-plane -- reach no login door: their hits are a plugin credential field and a connector secret. A new test in tests/gate-agent.test.mjs walks the whole family and fails any script that names a login path without either sending the header or being listed with its reason, so a gate that grows a login leg next month is a red test rather than a burst nobody can name. docs/ADMIN.md now carries the same list for the operator, with the sentence that matters: **an unlabelled burst is not automatically a stranger**, because every deploy-gate run before 2026-09-09 18:00 UTC predates the header | S |

| TITAN-CATALOG-1a · **landed on the branch and measured on grok-bot-local-vm 2026-09-09; not shipped to the R750 yet** | Jason, 17:40: "Titan should be able to see all connectors and all the agents as a catalog. Titan had filed the same thing himself at 17:38: he could create agents blank and read profile.json files, but "a template system where you pick a pre-built role (Instagram Marketer, Scribe, Research Agent, etc.) and it comes with a starter persona, memory seeds, and maybe connector configs, that's not here yet." He was right, and the reason was structural: the whole Add sequence lived in the browser, in `ui/machine-room/bot-setup.js`, and nothing in the box could run it. **Measured on grok-bot-local-vm 2026-09-09 before this wave:** a fresh agent asked "create me an Instagram marketer" made exactly one tool call, `CreateAgent`, and shipped an agent with a model-invented persona, 0 memories, 0 jobs and 0 playbooks. **What landed here (item 1a of the wave).** The eight-step sequence moved into the host as the gateway verb `importMarketplaceBot {id, name?, duplicate?}` (`source/host/extensions/marketplace/marketplace-bot-import.ts`), unchanged in order, idempotency, " copy" behaviour, rollback, persona composition, fact splitting, document naming, cron rule and plain-words receipt. It takes an ID and resolves the row itself, so a caller holding a list CARD cannot import a description-only agent holding nothing. `bot-setup.js` is 125 lines now: a roster read, one call, and two sentences a browser has to say for itself (a box older than the console, and a box that answers with nothing). The console and the agent are two doors onto one import. Item 1b — Titan's own catalog tools, the "template or from scratch" question in the standing persona, and the seed skill — is a separate builder and a separate row. **Measured on grok-bot-local-vm 2026-09-09, host bundle built from this branch, box restored to the bundle it was found on afterwards.** `scripts/verify-titan-catalog.mjs` against `account-book` (5 memories -> 7 facts, 11 playbooks, 3 jobs, 11 apps): the verb set it up in 212 ms (195-301 ms across three runs); all 7 facts stored whole, character for character; all 11 playbooks in the shared library; all 3 jobs on the box and every one switched off; the third job, which names no clock, reported with its reason. The same row through `bot-setup.js` loaded into the gate process made exactly one bot, and the two agents matched field for field — same facts, same 11 playbooks, same jobs, same app buckets — with the second import REUSING all 11 documents rather than doubling them. A third press answered `already` and wrote nothing. Every bot the gate made came off the roster. Full gate on the rebased tree: **34 PASS, 0 FAIL, 0 unmeasured, exit 0**, and the bot introduced itself in its own words 15 s after the import. That leg is worth reading twice: on two earlier runs the same box took the request (`isIntroductionInFlight` true) and wrote nothing inside 110 s and then 170 s, while another wave was driving the same box hard. So the leg checks two separate things — that the verb asks for the introduction LAST, after everything is seeded, which is this wave's to own, and then the words, which depend on the model behind the box and are reported as unmeasured rather than red when a busy box is slow. BOX-7 records that the R750 demo tenant's box starts no introduction for any new agent at all, which is why the words are not asserted there. | Owner: the ship. So `app.pluginId` was always empty, every `offer: "connect"` row fell past the connected and addable branches into the add-your-own bucket, and the receipt read "Slack, Notion, ... are not something we carry yet, so you would add your own" for apps this box carries and may already have installed. **Measured against the live host's own `getMarketplaceItem` answer for `account-book` on grok-bot-local-vm 2026-09-09:** 11 app rows served, 7 carrying a `plugin` id and 0 carrying a `pluginId`; the shipped code put all 11 in `byo`. Roughly 177 of the 244 app entries across the 65 community rows carry a plugin id and every one was misreported. **The second half:** `installedPluginIds` was an option NO caller in the product ever passed (its only other occurrences were two lines in tests/bot-setup.test.mjs), so `connected` was empty even on the six first-party integrations-only rows whose path otherwise worked. **Why the suite was green over it:** the fixture at tests/bot-setup.test.mjs wrote its apps in the REPORT's internal vocabulary (`pluginId`/`description`), which no catalog row has ever carried, and docs/BOTS.md documented that same bucket shape without noticing. **Found twice on the same evening, from both ends.** BOTS-4's own review wave measured it on the R750 demo tenant on one press of Add for `mr-toms` and fixed the console's reader by normalising both spellings in `appsOf`; this wave measured it against the live host's answer on grok-bot-local-vm and moved the reading into the box. The two are not a duplicate: theirs keeps the page honest for as long as the page reads a row itself, and this one is the reader both doors go through. **A third field, found while rebasing onto their fix:** the generator writes an empty `line` on a row whose sentence another bot wrote first and keeps the shared one under `fallbackLine` — `account-book` serves `"line": ""` for Gmail and Google Calendar — so a reader that stops at `line` hands a person an app name with nothing under it. The host reader takes `line`, then `fallbackLine`, then `description`, which is what the Bots tab's own reader does. **Fixed in the move rather than ported:** the host verb reads `plugin` and `line` first with the old spellings as fallbacks, and DERIVES installed state itself through the reader the plugin tools use (connectors.json for a connector, a binary probe for a shell tool) rather than accepting it as an argument, so the omission cannot recur. Only names and booleans leave that module. **Measured after the fix, same box and row:** offered `Slack, Notion, Linear, Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Drive, Google Sheets` (the 7 that name a plugin), unavailable `Hex, Databricks SQL, Granola, Salesforce` (the 4 that name none). The regression case is fed `findMarketplaceBot("account-book")` straight out of the bundled catalog, never a hand-written fixture. | Owner: closed by TITAN-CATALOG-1a. Next action: none for the import. The rule that a check over an app row is fed a real catalog row rather than a fixture is written into docs/BOTS.md's "What bites". |

| GATE-17 · **found and fixed inside TITAN-CATALOG-1a's own gate, 2026-09-09** | Two ways a gate can measure itself rather than the product, both hit in one evening on grok-bot-local-vm. (1) **The transcript has no role of "assistant".** An agent's own words are an entry of `kind: "send-message"` carrying `message.content`. A gate leg looking for a role of assistant waited out its full 110 s ceiling against a box that had already spoken and reported the product broken. (2) **A roster count is not a gate's to assert on a shared box.** The cleanup leg asserted `roster.length === rosterCount` and went red because another wave was driving the same box without `scripts/on-box.sh` and its scratch agents came and went under the run. The correct assertion is that the bots THIS gate made are off the roster, which is what verify-bots.mjs's own header already says ("diffed, never counted"). Both are fixed in scripts/verify-titan-catalog.mjs, and the second is written into its header so the next gate does not repeat it. A third, caught before it reached a commit: this wave's new host suite was first written to `tests/marketplace-bot-import.test.mjs`, a name BOTS-4 already used for the Bots tab page's own `importBot`, and creating it destroyed that file. It was restored from HEAD and the new suite is `tests/host-marketplace-bot-import.test.mjs`. On a branch four waves share, check the working tree for a file you believe you are creating before you believe it. | Owner: closed. Next action: none. Worth a glance from anyone writing a new verify-*.mjs — grep the other gates for `role` on a transcript entry and for a roster-count equality. |

| PROXY-1 · **LANDED and measured on the R750 2026-09-08** | Jason, 2026-09-07 13:38 and 17:11: the product ships with a basic set of provider subscriptions held by the super admin (Z.AI, TinyFish first) proxied to every customer and metered; customers may still bring their own keys; he holds TWO subscriptions each for Z.AI and TinyFish, and today's console can hold only one key per provider (one endpoint row per provider id in ui/subscriptions.mjs) | Owned, after ADMIN-1 and AUTH-MFA-1. Decision 2026-09-07 19:20 after looking at the field: NVIDIA PAIR is a LAN inference router (no keys, no tenants), Hermes' subscription proxy is a one-credential local passthrough, LiteLLM's AI Gateway already does the core (self-hosted, OpenAI-compatible, virtual keys per customer with budgets and rate limits, spend tracking, several keys of one provider pooled with fallback, an admin API, an MCP passthrough). Plan: run LiteLLM as a Coolify service beside the control plane on the R750 with Postgres; the control plane mints one virtual key per box with its plan budget and stores only that in the box (box-secrets points at the gateway); the operator's provider keys and any bring-your-own key live only in the gateway's config; the admin console reads spend per client from the gateway; revoking a client deletes its virtual key; TinyFish rides the gateway's MCP passthrough or a small passthrough of ours; observe mode first, then plan allowances with a warning at 80 percent and a plain stop; Settings shows "Included with your plan" and "Your own keys"; Jason confirmed 19:22 that Z.AI's coding plan, MiniMax and Qwen are plain API keys (his vault: Anthropic, Z.AI with base https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4, OpenAI, Gemini, MiniMax, NVIDIA, Qwen, IFM K2 Horizon for inference; TinyFish; ElevenLabs, Deepgram, HeyGen, FAL; Context7; Resend), so LiteLLM carries them directly and a refreshing adapter is only needed for a login-based subscription such as Codex, later; the allowed-path list and no body logging are kept from the Hermes pattern. **The design is written up in `docs/PROXY.md`. The TinyFish half of it is built and measured; the model half, the mint, the console and the R750 ship are the other three builders and the integrator. MEASURED ON THIS MAC (Darwin 25.5.0, node 22.23.1, Docker 29.5.3), 2026-09-08, against a real LiteLLM v1.100.0 with Postgres 16 and stub upstreams — no real TinyFish key was available to the measurement, so these are the proxy hop and not TinyFish's own time.** The REST pass-through is the metered route and does everything the wave asks of it: `/tinyfish/fetch` answered in 16 ms and `/tinyfish/search` in 8 ms; the upstream received the OPERATOR key in `x-api-key` (44 chars, sha256 `18927beb`) and the calling box's virtual key appeared in nothing it received; the query string survived the hop; spend landed at 0.004 on the calling key after the batch window with 0 on a second tenant's key minted beside it; and a deleted key was refused 0.0 s after `/key/delete`. The MCP mount carries `mcp-remote` 0.8.4 unchanged (connect, initialize, `tools/list`, about 1 s from spawn), so the wave's named fallback did not fire and leg one ships — but it does not meter (PROXY-4), a plain virtual key sees an EMPTY tool list with HTTP 200 and no error unless it is minted with `object_permission: {mcp_servers: [...]}` (PROXY-5), and the mount renames the tools to `tinyfish-search` / `tinyfish-fetch_content` while still resolving the unprefixed name on a call. Unit proof on this Mac: `tinyfish-key-stub` 9/9 with three new route cases, `connector-tinyfish-preset` 11/11 with two new preset cases, `web-tools-ours` 31/31 **unchanged** so the CURSOR-1 fallback order and words are pinned, `marketplace-catalog` 15/15, source typecheck clean, `verify-cursor-free` 16 PASS 0 FAIL and `verify-browser-tools` 106 PASS against `grok-bot-local-vm`. **Not measured, and owed by the integrator:** everything on the R750 — a real TinyFish key behind the pass-through, Richard's Titan fetching a page through it, the operator key absent from his box proved by name, mode and sha256 prefix, and the revocation seconds **CLOSED OUT ON THE R750 2026-09-08, 04:40 to 05:20 UTC, from this Mac (Darwin 25.5.0, node 22.23.1) against the R750 (Coolify 4, docker 29).** The four worktrees were merged, made to agree, and shipped in the order the ship plan set: the proxy service, the control plane, the relay, the host bundle, then the migration. **On this Mac:** `npm test` **1337 pass 0 fail** (1258 before the wave), `source:typecheck` clean, `verify-proxy` against the stub **25 pass 0 fail 1 inconclusive** (the TinyFish leg, which measures a credential swap the stub does not model and says so), the tenant leg **13 of 13**. **On the R750:** `titanbot-proxy` and `titanbot-proxy-db` run as a Coolify service on `titanbot-net` with **no published port** (`docker port` prints nothing), no Domain and no traefik label; `/health/readiness` answers `{"status":"healthy","db":"connected"}` from inside the relay; `box-isolation.sh --verify` **PASS** with all three boxes reaching the proxy on 4000, none reaching 5432, and no box reaching another box; `/v1/models` serves exactly `plan-zai, plan-minimax`. **The three boxes before:** one byte-identical `box-secrets.json`, 539 bytes, **mode 0644**, holding the same operator provider key (113 chars, sha256 `734e60c2f9de`). **After:** 365 bytes, **mode 0600**, `734e60c2` gone from all three, each holding its own 25-char virtual key (`dc589bd09059` demo — `d071e05c0cbf` was demo's FIRST key and was revoked and re-minted at 05:12:54 while the migration hazard below was being fixed; `cef1d10b0a5b` richard-avery, `69b9e1a049d8` titanium), all pointed at `http://titanbot-proxy:4000/v1` on `plan-zai` with `SERVED_BY` set. **Proofs:** every box answered a real prompt through the proxy from inside itself, on its own key (2.6 s, 2.0 s, 3.2 s); the operator's old key presented at the proxy is **401** and the refusal carries no key; the admin spend panel shows all three clients with both windows and no refusal; **revocation was refused at the proxy 0.1 s after `/key/delete`**, well inside the minute the design asks for, and the tenant was re-minted and answering again. **Six defects the live server found, each fixed with a test:** the mint carried `tags`, which is enterprise-only and 403s, so provisioning could never have succeeded; it lacked `object_permission`, without which a customer's TinyFish connector reports healthy with an empty tool list; the MCP config named `authorization_token`, a field that does not exist (the model calls it `authentication_token`); `CP_PROXY_URL` already carries `/v1`, so every mint was going to `/v1/key/generate` and every box to `/v1/v1`; the whole spend panel was built on `/global/spend/report`, also enterprise-only, and its refusal rendered as `[object Object]`; and **Jason's own workspace was the one box the migration could not reach**, because the control plane drops the operator's row over a gateway token it deliberately does not hold, and the virtual key rides on that row. **Two hazards closed while shipping:** `sync.sh` ran `install.sh` by default and built a second standalone relay and box beside the live fleet (removed; it now stops when it sees a Coolify-managed relay); and `proxy migrate` wrote the key the RELAY held, which lags a re-mint by a refresh cycle, so a revoke-then-migrate put a **revoked** key into a box and reported success (it now compares hashes and deletes nothing until they agree). **Still owed, and named rather than implied:** the TinyFish copy (`9165ce2daa86`) is STILL in all three boxes and is deliberately left there — there is no TinyFish name in `~/.api_keys` and the operator's key lives in `~/.tinyfish`, which this work may not read, so `PROXY_TINYFISH_KEY_1` is unset and the pass-through has no credential; removing the copy would break TinyFish with no replacement. That is `PROXY-7`. The browser-side customer walk (sign in as Richard, see the Included cards, click Use this one) was not run; the box-level proof was taken instead. **AUDITED AND CORRECTED 2026-09-08, and the wave's central claim was false as first written.** "734e60c2 gone from all three" was proved by reading three files, and the key was not gone: `box-store-sync` had already copied `box-secrets.json` into each box's own content-addressed store, so a byte-identical 539-byte copy of the same 113-character key sat at `/var/lib/sand-box-store/<store id>/blobs/<sha256>`, mode 0644 root:root, in **all three boxes** — and the agent host runs as root inside the box, so any shell tool call a customer's agent made could read it. Four more defects the same audit found and this pass fixed: the two `pass_through_endpoints` entries were at the TOP LEVEL of `config.yaml` and LiteLLM reads that key from `general_settings` alone, so the TinyFish REST route was parsed, discarded and answered 404 on the R750 while `docs/PROXY.md` §7 carried a measured table for it; `use-included` rewrote the rollback snapshot on every call, so demo's second migration saved a REVOKED proxy key as its own way back and `titanium` had no snapshot at all; no tenant key carried a rate limit or a hard budget (`max_budget`, `tpm_limit`, `rpm_limit`, `max_parallel_requests` all NULL in the proxy's Postgres, `CP_PROXY_RPM_LIMIT` unset on `titanbot-cp`), so nothing stopped one customer consuming the pooled subscription; and a tenant key could call `GET /health`, which makes a live call to every provider deployment on the operator's subscriptions — one admin sweep of it left three rows in `/spend/logs` under `litellm-internal-health-check` at $0.000043, charged to the operator and attributed to nobody. **What landed for each:** the host no longer offers `box-secrets.json` or `connector-env-secrets.json` to the box store (`source/host/durable-file-policy.ts`) and `forget-provider-keys` now sweeps the store, deleting stored copies of those two documents and NAMING every other store file that still carries the value rather than deleting a pack or a conversation database to chase a credential; the pass-through block moved under `general_settings` and the gate now asks the running proxy whether the route exists; the snapshot is written once and never overwritten, with a `WAY BACK` column on `proxy list`; `proxy limits` applies budget and rate limit to keys that already exist; and `general_settings.allowed_routes` closes `/health` and everything else the product does not call. **The one thing code cannot do: the Alibaba/Qwen key `734e60c2f9de` sat readable-to-root inside two customers' boxes and has to be ROTATED at the vendor.** That is Jason's, and it is the only thing that ends it | L |

The plugin page draws ONE masked box per credential with its hint and the line "Stored once. Used by the connector and by the agent's shell." The second card is gone. GitHub is the case the two-card shape could never have handled: one pasted token lands as `GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` for the connector and `GITHUB_TOKEN` for the shell. No box has a proxy mount configured, so every box today gets the same catalog it always did. **Measured on this Mac:** `tests/plugin-credential-fanout.test.mjs`, 10 cases, green. **Measured on the R750 demo box, 2026-09-08, through console.titanium.bot:** the TinyFish page draws one credential block | S |
| TOOLS-FETCH-1 · **part (2) closed, (1) and (3) open** | Richard's first session (2026-09-07 16:48 to 17:23): six links (YouTube, a Yahoo article, a Google Sheet, Instagram, LinkedIn, two Facebook pages), six refusals. The outline shows Titan called webFetchToolCall 11 times and webSearchToolCall 8 times and every call failed, because both built-in tools route through the Cursor backend. CURSOR-1 located the root cause: `inference-service.ts:57` returns `{ ...cursor, createSession, createSummarizationSession }`, and `createWebSearch`/`createWebFetch` are not in that override list, so they come through the spread from the Cursor object whatever provider the operator picked. The backend is not retired either: measured 2026-09-07, `api2.cursor.sh` answers 200 from inside a box and the RPC 401s; his own notes say "the web search tool appears to be consistently failing". TinyFish never entered the picture (its connector was broken, MARKET-5). He never opened his desktop browser for any of them, though it works (he opened the TinyFish sign-in page in it at 17:23), and he fetched the Yahoo article and the YouTube page successfully with curl in his shell later in the same session while still telling Richard fetch was "glitching". Nothing told him the fallback order, and the tool's failure text names no alternative | Owned, three parts. (1) Now, done by hand on the three R750 boxes: Titan's standing role names the fallback order (own browser, then TinyFish, then the shell, and never asking the person to paste a public page). (2) **Landed 2026-09-07 (CURSOR-1).** `createWebSearch` / `createWebFetch` in `source/host/extensions/inference/production.ts` now build `source/host/extensions/inference/web-tools.ts` instead of the two `AiService.RunWebFetch` / `RunWebSearch` clients, and `cursor-web-tools.ts` is deleted. WebFetch reads the page from the box itself (browser-like User-Agent, redirects followed, 25 MB cap, HTML reduced to text) and falls through to TinyFish only when the site refuses: a non-2xx, a body that reduces to nothing because the page only draws itself in a browser, or content that is not text. The fallback is the `tinyfish` connector's `fetch_content` through the box's own MCP client when that connector is installed, and otherwise `POST https://api.fetch.tinyfish.ai` with the key already in `connector-env-secrets.json`; WebSearch answers through `search` and `GET https://api.search.tinyfish.ai` by the same route. Both tool names, schemas, renderers and console tool rows are unchanged. When everything fails the result says what was tried and names the next move ("Open the page in your browser and read it from there"), and no failure text says "may be temporary" or names a tool, a service or a vendor. The system prompt gained "Say the outcome, not the tool" so the person never hears a tool name in a reply unless they ask how it was done. `tests/web-tools-ours.test.mjs` pins the order, the two routes and the words, against a fake TinyFish and an invented key. (3) Onboarding (ONBOARD-1) tells the person in plain words that Titan can open any site in his own browser, so "open it in your browser" is a phrase they know **Part (2) closed 2026-09-07, measured on grok-bot-local-vm** with the box cut off from Cursor two ways at once: a turn asking for the heading of https://example.com came back "The heading on example.com is \"Example Domain\"", a turn asking for the first thing on https://www.linkedin.com/company/anthropic (a page that walls a plain fetch) came back with that page's own "Agree & Join" text, and a WebSearch on a box with no backup configured answered "Could not run that search. No reply in either turn carried "may be temporary", a vendor name or a tool name. `scripts/verify-cursor-free.mjs`: 16 PASS, 0 FAIL. | FEEDBACK-1 · **LANDED and measured on the R750 2026-09-09, wave C** | Jason, 2026-09-07 18:03: "Titan tried to cover up failure. It then sends it in to us... it should come in somewhere and then become a GitHub issue." | Owned, after ADMIN-1 (it adds a panel there). (1) Persona, **NOT SHIPPED IN THE PRODUCT PROMPT AND NOT WAVE C'S TO SHIP**: the standing role should say that a failed tool is named plainly (which tool, what it answered, what is tried next), that a failure is never called temporary unless it has succeeded before, that a workaround is never presented as success, and that the owner is never asked to do by hand what a tool can do until every tool was tried. `source/host/runner/system-prompt.ts` is wave A's file and carries none of that, checked in the merged tree 2026-09-09. The sentence itself is written out in `docs/FEEDBACK.md` section 8 for wave A to paste verbatim, and the row that owns it is **FEEDBACK-1c**. Set by hand on the R750 boxes today, product-wide with PERSONA-1. (2) Console: a Report a problem control always present, and an offer that appears on its own after a failed turn or after the same tool fails three times in one conversation: "Would you like to send this to the developers?" with the report shown before sending (the failing tool calls and their answers, the agent's last messages, the workspace and console version; never a secret; the person can edit it. **No screenshot:** the design named an optional one and it is not built, because a PNG is the one piece of evidence the page's masker cannot read before it is sent, so carrying one would break the card's own custody line. Recorded in `docs/FEEDBACK.md` and owned as FEEDBACK-1d). (3) Control plane: POST /v1/feedback stores it; the admin console's Feedback panel lists reports with the workspace, time and status; a Create GitHub issue action files it in the product repo with the report as the body through a repo token held by the control plane, and a triage state (new, issue filed, closed) so nothing lands on GitHub unread Also: Titan's own self-test checklist (saved by Jason's Titan as the workflow "tool-self-test", 2026-09-07) becomes a Run a self-test action in the console that produces the same report and offers to send it. **Landed as three pieces with no control-plane credential anywhere inside a customer's box: the agent's tool writes a pending report into the box's own store and posts nothing; the console (already the tenant's own session) is the only thing that POSTs, through a new relay door that stamps the workspace from the relay's own registry and ignores any slug in the body; the control plane stores it and the admin console's seventh panel is the super admin's gate.** Measured end to end on the R750 through https://console.titanium.bot signed in as the demo customer, 2026-09-09: the demo bot called the tool, the transcript drew exactly one muted row reading "Reported a problem to the developers" with the tool's name nowhere on the page (asserted against the rendered text), the card came up with the agent's own title, its tier chip, the custody line and an editable body, the person added a line and pressed Send, one POST left the page, and the report was listed at https://api.titanium.bot/admin within one reload as tier critical, workspace demo, state new. Approve wrote `approved` with who and when. **Create GitHub issue answered, in exactly these words, "the issue body is ready; paste a repo token in the Feedback panel and press this again"** with the whole issue body rendered and zero requests sent anywhere: the door is proven and unfired, because no repository token has been pasted yet, and none is ever pushed into a box (every exec daemon runs as uid 0, so a super-admin repo token inside a tenant container is readable by that customer's agents). The automatic offer was measured on the same console the same day on a genuinely failed turn (BOX-6 transcript corruption on the demo Titan): the card appeared, was sent, and is report #1 in the panel. Local proof on grok-bot-local-vm at bundle df1300366eb2: a scratch agent called the tool for real and the pending file carried ProblemReport v1 with the agent's own title (`verify-feedback --agent`, 4/4); the console arc with the POST body captured in real Chrome is `verify-feedback` (27/27); the control plane and the panel are `verify-admin` (300 PASS, 0 FAIL, on this Mac). **Four things a review of this row's own code found and wave C then fixed, each measured on this Mac 2026-09-09 at the merged tree.** (a) **The masking was in the wrong place and a customer's key could leave the workspace.** `reportRedact` ran at DRAW time on the evidence the page built for itself, so an agent-written description or a tool's answer quoting a key was starred on screen and sent RAW: measured, the card showed `sk-A…[redacted, 35 chars]` while the wire carried `sk-A1b2C3d4E5f6G7h8I9j0K1l2M3n4O5p6`, and driving the real admin api with a stubbed GitHub the planted key reached the issue POST body. Masking now happens ONCE at mint time in `offerProblemReport` over the title, description, steps, tool answers and evidence, so card, body and payload are the same bytes; pinned by a `tests/machine-room-feedback.test.mjs` case that fails against the old code. (b) **The intake truncated a report the console legitimately minted and answered 201.** An ordinary shell-failure report is 15,296 characters, of which 8,000 were stored; two of the twelve calls were dropped and each call's output cut from 1,200 to 800, with nothing on any screen saying so. `LIMITS` are now the console's own maxima (description 32,000, twelve calls, 1,200-character outputs) and anything over one is REFUSED with a sentence naming the field instead of cut; the feedback route on both the relay and the control plane reads up to `INTAKE_BYTES` (96 KB) because a report carries its evidence twice, and a report at every limit at once is 65,671 bytes. (c) **A suppressed report could be filed, which overwrote the suppression on the row the panel draws**; filing now refuses a suppressed or closed report in a sentence naming who decided and when, and filing a report still in `new` is recorded as the approval it is. Two new legs in `scripts/verify-admin.mjs`. (d) **Every plain sentence the relay wrote for a failed send was thrown away**: the console read `body.error` and the relay only ever sets `message`, so the person read "the report was not accepted (503)". Measured in real Chrome by a new `verify-feedback` leg, the card now reads the relay's own sentence with no digit in it. **All four shipped and re-measured on the R750 after the ship at bundle 9276b2bc72af, 2026-09-09** (control plane rebuilt and restarted through Coolify, relay restarted last; no box touched, because the host bundle built from this commit is byte-identical to the one all three boxes already run, sha 6ae3565fbfe09553). Against the live https://api.titanium.bot: a report of the shape the console really mints -- 19,385 characters of description, twelve calls, each with a 1,200-character output, 39,870 bytes on the wire -- answered 201 and is stored WHOLE (description 19,385 of 19,385, twelve calls of twelve, output 1,200 of 1,200), where the same report before this fix would have been stored as 8,000 characters and ten calls with 800-character outputs and still answered 201. A 40,000-character description answered 400 with "the description is 40000 characters and at most 32000 are carried. Nothing was stored". That report was then suppressed and Create GitHub issue answered 409, "Report 3 was suppressed on 2026-09-09 by the operator token, so nothing was filed. Approve it again first if that decision has changed", with the row still listed under the suppressed filter afterwards. The console half is served from the relay as the same bytes this commit carries (sha 07011d5d557e0b91 for app.js and 70aa3ace8924cd96 for gateway-adapter.js, equal on the server, in the relay's container and in the commit), and its behaviour is measured in real Chrome on this Mac by `verify-feedback` (30/30, including the card reading the relay's own sentence with no digit in it) and by `verify-admin` (browser leg included, 0 FAIL). Gaps left, each its own row below: FEEDBACK-1b (the pending file is read only at page load), FEEDBACK-1c (the persona sentence is still wave A's to paste) and FEEDBACK-1d (no screenshot on the card) | M |

Now (small, host writer): box-secrets.json written 0600 like its neighbours. With PROXY-1: no provider key inside a box at all, plan keys AND bring-your-own keys held by the control plane and used through the metering proxy, the box holding only a per-box proxy credential that can be revoked from the admin console. **Where this stands after PROXY-1's TinyFish half.** The mechanism for the TinyFish copy is in and measured on this Mac: a box reads its credential and its two endpoints from one 0600 file, the pass-through adds the operator's key on the far side, and the box's own key reached nothing upstream in the measured run. **What is still owed is the removal itself**, which is the security work and which only the R750 can prove: the migration must leave `9165ce2d` (the operator TinyFish key, 44 chars) absent from every customer box's `connector-env-secrets.json` and `734e60c2` (the operator provider key, 113 chars) absent from `box-secrets.json`, each proved by name, mode and sha256 prefix rather than by assertion. Its second half — bring-your-own keys held by the control plane rather than by the box — is deliberately NOT in this wave, because Jason's wave says bring-your-own stays exactly as today; it is now **PROXY-2** with an owner and a next action **Measured on the R750 2026-09-08:** all three boxes now hold `box-secrets.json` at **mode 0600** (was 0644 box:box on every one) and the copied operator provider key `734e60c2f9de` was removed from that file in all three, reported by name, length and sha256 prefix only. **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY: it was not gone from the BOX.** That proof read three files; `box-store-sync` had already copied `box-secrets.json` into each box's own content-addressed store, so a byte-identical 539-byte copy of the same key sat at `/var/lib/sand-box-store/<store id>/blobs/<sha256>`, mode 0644 root:root, in all three, readable by the agent host, which runs as root inside the box. **Landed in the correction pass:** the host never offers `box-secrets.json` or `connector-env-secrets.json` to the store again (`BOX_STORE_SECRET_FILE_NAMES` in `source/host/durable-file-policy.ts`, pinned by `tests/box-store-secret-exclusion.test.mjs`), and `forget-provider-keys` sweeps the store, deletes stored copies of those two documents and NAMES every other store file that still carries the value rather than deleting a pack or a conversation database to chase a credential. **What no code change can do: that key sat readable-to-root inside two customers' boxes and has to be ROTATED at the vendor.** Jason's, and the only thing that ends it. **MEASURED ON THE R750 2026-09-08 06:20-06:35 UTC, before and after.** Before, in every one of the three boxes: `/var/lib/sand-box-store/<store id>/blobs/b70ddfdbbdd8735faf85373dd08e7fd9a6dd7146e0d1773cf6f60680006f47ad`, **mode 0644 root:root, 539 bytes**, holding a value 113 characters long whose sha256 starts `734e60c2f9de`. The three boxes were taken to host `611fc9caae4a` one at a time (`post-swap watch disarmed: host up 60s ... (healthy)` on each), then `forget-provider-keys` was run per tenant through the relay's admin door: **`removedCount 1, storeSwept true, storeCarrying 0`** on demo, richard-avery and titanium. After: that blob path does not exist in any box, and a full hash sweep of Richard's whole box volume (2795 files under `sand-data`, the store, `/workspace` and `cli-config`, hashing every printable run of 44 and 113 characters) reports **`734e60c2f9de: ABSENT`**. All three boxes then answered a real prompt through the proxy on their own virtual key (**2.3 s** richard-avery, **1.8 s** titanium). `connector-env-secrets.json` is 0600 as well. **What is NOT done:** the copied operator TinyFish key `9165ce2daa86` is still in all three boxes, because the proxy has no TinyFish credential to replace it with — see PROXY-7 | S / M |
| CLOUD-BROWSER-1 · **landed** (2026-09-09) | The four browser tools drove the Chrome inside the box and nothing else, so a page that refuses a datacentre address refused the agent, a saved login died with the box, and a step only a person can do had no way to reach one | **Landed. The cloud leg is a branch inside `SandBrowserDriver.run()`, driven by the SAME dependency-free CDP client the box uses**, so the four tool names, their specs and `turn-toolset.ts` are untouched and the result shape is identical by construction rather than by two implementations agreeing -- the same page-text extractor, the same JPEG-at-1280 pipeline, the same verdicts, and the same second address guard that does a real DNS lookup and refuses a public name resolving to a private address. Custody moved with it: the request travels on stdin or in a 0600 file under a 0700 directory the driver unlinks as it reads, never in argv, and the gate greps every process argument list after a run. **Measured on `grok-bot-local-vm`:** the driver run twice on the same page, once with display+cdpPort and once with `cdpUrl`, returned an IDENTICAL field set (example.com, 129 characters of text each way); no vendor host, key or session id in any of 282 process argument lists; zero request files left behind; `verify-browser-tools` 108 PASS / 0 FAIL on the full run and 42 checks on the dry run. **Measured on the R750 demo tenant, the customer's way:** the Browser Use key typed into the masked box on its Marketplace page reached the host's own store (`getCloudBrowserPolicy` answers `available:["browser-use"]`, names only, no value anywhere on the page); an agent asked for `instagram.com/titaniumcomputing/` was routed to the cloud engine on reason "this site is on the workspace's cloud list", TWO sessions, both opened before the connect and both closed, 0.09 and 0.135 minutes, and BOTH read back from the vendor afterwards as `state: stopped`. The ledger row is `{tenant, agentId, vendor, sessionId, startedAt, endedAt, minutes, proxyBytes|null, engine, reason, url}` at 0600, and the super admin's Marketplace panel draws it with the workspace named `demo` (the relay stamps the authoritative slug over the box's own name for itself), 2 sessions, 0.2 min, and "not reported by this vendor" where Browser Use publishes no traffic figure -- never a zero, because a zero on a money screen reads as free. **The new `emptyShell` verdict is the reason the routing works at all** and it fired on the R750 in plain words: the page came back with its title and Meta's footer and the agent said "This page loaded but gave up almost none of its words ... try another source or ask the person to look at the screen" instead of summarising a page it had not read. MACHINE: measured on the R750 (`titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau`, slug demo) through console.titanium.bot and api.titanium.bot in a real browser, on bundle `d5a47bcc1d72`, 2026-09-09; the local legs on `grok-bot-local-vm` on this Mac the same day. | M |

| CLOUD-BROWSER-4 · **fixed** (2026-09-09) | A cloud browser was minted and stopped inside one tool call, so nothing multi-step could ever happen in one -- which is every job this wave exists for: a sign-up, a login, a code typed on a second page | **The browser is now held for the life of the page, not of the call.** `CloudBrowserService` keeps a map of which browser holds which page and the driver asks it BEFORE it routes: an op that carries no address never routes at all and goes to whichever browser has the page, so the two engines can no longer interleave inside one. It is deliberately NOT released at a turn boundary: the hand-off card ends the bot's turn on purpose, and closing there would throw the session away in the exact moment a person is about to take it over. **Measured on the R750 demo tenant, the customer's way:** an agent asked for `instagram.com/titaniumcomputing/` and then for one fresh picture of the same page made ONE cloud session, `69d3afd1`, opened 20:06:10.512Z on reason "this site is on the workspace's cloud list" -- against TWO sessions five seconds apart for the same page before the fix; the turn's own wire lines carry `imageParts` 0 then 1 then 2, so both browser calls came back with a picture from the one browser; and the idle timer gave it back at 20:10:25.117Z, 4.243 min, stopped at the vendor. **Measured on `grok-bot-local-vm`:** `verify-browser-tools` on the box path 111 PASS / 0 FAIL; on the cloud path (`--cloud-shape`, the box's own Chrome behind the whole cloud road) ONE ledger row for a plan of eleven tool calls where the same plan used to write one per call, every call at the same endpoint, and the held browser given back by the idle timer within 8 s of nothing using it -- a leg the gate now runs, because the old assertion ("every session closed") passed on exactly the bug. MACHINE: the R750 demo box `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau` on bundle `209b3833c371`, and `grok-bot-local-vm` on this Mac, both 2026-09-09. | M |

| PROXY-8 · **CLOSED and measured on the R750** (2026-09-08 19:36Z) | Any tenant key can read any other tenant's key record from the proxy. From inside `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau`, authenticated with demo's own virtual key, `GET /key/info?key=<richard-avery's key hash>` answered **200** with `{"key_alias":"titanbot-richard-avery","spend":0.0,"models":["plan-zai","plan-minimax"]}`. The hash is 64 hex and is not guessable, so it needs a leak to exploit, but the authorisation check is simply absent and the tenant slug is in the alias. `general_settings.allowed_routes` CANNOT close it: the check runs in `pre_db_read_auth_checks` before the key is looked up, so it is one global list that refuses the master key too, and `cp/admin.mjs` calls `/key/info` itself. `GET /model/info` is open to a tenant key for the same reason (200, the full model list and every `api_base`; no key material, checked) | Owner: the pass after PROXY-1's correction. Next action, in order: (1) check whether a later LiteLLM authorises `/key/info` against the presenting key — this is v1.100.0's behaviour, not a stated design; (2) failing that, stop calling `/key/info` from `cp/admin.mjs` (the spend panel already reads `/spend/logs`, and `keyInfo` is the only other caller), take `/key/info` and `/model/info` off `allowed_routes`, and have the control plane serve the two facts it needs from its own ledger. Neither is urgent on its own; both stop being possible the moment (2) lands. **PROVIDERS-1 answers this by moving the boundary from the global list to the KEY.** A global list cannot tell a tenant from the operator, which is exactly why this row is open, and it cannot express the path-parameter routes the Providers panel needs either. The replacement is `allowed_routes` on each virtual key at mint plus a backfill over every key already in the field — and the ORDER is load bearing: keys in the field were minted with `allowed_routes []` and are unrestricted, so the backfill lands BEFORE the global list is removed or there is a window where the admin surface is open to every box on the bridge. `/key/info` stays in `cp/admin.mjs` called with the MASTER key, which a per-key list does not touch. Measured on this Mac 2026-09-08 against LiteLLM v1.100.0 with no `Authorization` header at all, `/public/providers`, `/public/providers/fields`, `/public/model_hub`, `/public/litellm_model_cost_map` and `/health/readiness` answered 200 while `/model/info` and `/key/info` answered 401 — so the door list never closed everything, and docs/PROXY.md §8 now says so. The per-key 403 and the backfill are the proxy and control-plane items of PROVIDERS-1 and are measured there, on the R750 | S | **How it closed.** Not by widening the global door list, which is checked in `pre_db_read_auth_checks` before the key is looked up and therefore cannot tell a tenant from the operator, and which an exact string match could not express `PATCH /credentials/{name}` in anyway. The boundary moved onto the key. Every key already in the field was minted with `allowed_routes []`, so the order was load bearing and was kept: `node cp/cli.mjs proxy limits --all` pushed `[/v1/chat/completions, /chat/completions, /v1/models, /models]` onto all three tenant keys FIRST (measured: `applied, key value unchanged` for titanium, demo and richard-avery, and `POST /key/update` does take `allowed_routes` on an existing key, so nothing was written into any box), and only then did `general_settings.allowed_routes` come out of `config.yaml` at the stage-one restart. MEASURED from inside `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau` with demo's own key, AFTER the global list was gone: `/key/info`, `/model/info`, `/model_group/info` and `/health` all **403**, `GET /v1/models` **200** listing the three plan models, `POST /v1/chat/completions` **200**. The master key reading the same records from `titanbot-cp` is unaffected. `/health` was the door list's real job and it is closed per key now, which is what stops a tenant-triggered health sweep spending the operator's money.

Measured on the R750 2026-09-08 with a scanner that hashes candidates and reports names and prefixes only: 2 lines in that agent's 352, across 16 ledgers on that box; the demo box scanned clean across 87 ledgers. The outline's shell rows carry the same command and are read by the console, so a ledger-only fix would have left the credential on a screen | **Landed.** `source/host/secret-redaction.ts` reads the box's own two secret stores (`box-secrets.json`, `connector-env-secrets.json`), walks every string in them rather than two known shapes, and replaces any occurrence in the text with `<redacted:<first 12 of its sha256>>` — readable receipt, comparable hash. Wired at write time into `localAuditJsonlLine` (the shell command and the browser URL, since a token travels in a query string) and into the outline's shell row in `conversation-outline.ts`. **The length floor is the design decision:** 12 characters or more with no whitespace, so a model name, a context window or a boolean in the same file does not get redacted and mangle every row. Cached against mtime, never throws, and is the identity on a machine with no box. `tests/audit-secret-redaction.test.mjs` pins it with a 44-character fake key. Note in `docs/ADMIN.md` that ledger lines older than this change predate it. **There was a fourth writer into that same file and it was still open, found and closed 2026-09-09.** `agents/<id>/audit.jsonl` is written by the action auditor *and* by `source/host/extensions/evidence/evidence-registry.ts`, which appends a `tool_result` record for every work tool carrying up to `MAX_ATTESTATION_HEAD_CHARS` (8,000) of the head of the tool's RESULT. For a shell tool that is the command's own output. So the ledger was half redacted: the command line clean, and whatever that command printed going down in full — a turn that echoes a stored secret wrote it to disk, in the very file this row is about. Only the persisted copy is redacted, deliberately: `evidence-verdict` decides a claim by looking for its tokens inside `attestation.head` and the job bus reads the same heads, so redacting the in-memory attestation would change what the host concludes about a turn rather than what it writes down, and `bytes`/`sha256` keep measuring the original because an attestation of a redacted copy attests nothing. **Measured end to end on the demo box `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau` (R750), on the bundle carrying the fix, with a real agent turn running a real shell command:** a fake 44-character key was added to that box's own `connector-env-secrets.json`, the agent was asked to `printf %s "$HARDEN1_PROBE_KEY"`, and the new `tool_result` row reads `"stdout":"<redacted:48b12c232076>"` with the fake key appearing nowhere in `audit.jsonl` and nowhere in `audit-outbox.json`. The secret store was restored byte for byte afterwards (sha256 prefix `16e8280b2c85` before and after, 209 bytes, mode 0600, owner box). A scan of that box's history, printing counts only, found the operator's real key in 0 of 1,240 ledger lines, so the demo box never happened to echo it and Jason's box is where it landed. **Proved against the pre-fix tree rather than taken on trust:** reverting only the redaction call while keeping the exports turns the new leg red with `the evidence ledger still carries the key` and leaves the other six green **Two more writers, found 2026-09-09 and closed.** (5) *The screen this row was written about.* Only the standalone `shellConversationTurn` branch of `conversation-outline.ts` was redacted. The path an agent's own shell command takes -- `agentConversationTurn` -> `toolCall` -> `shellToolCall` -> `stepToOutlineItem` -> `shellOutline` -- put the raw command in `summary` and the raw stdout in `output` with no redactor anywhere, which is exactly what the file's own comment said would leave the credential on a screen. Measured on grok-bot-local-vm: one conversation's 1,578-item outline held 54 `shellToolCall` rows and all 54 carried a populated `output`. The redaction now lives inside `shellOutline` itself, the one seam both callers pass through, and inside `getToolCallActivityArgs`, which serializes every non-shell tool's arguments whole -- an MCP call or a browser navigate with a token in its arguments landed on the same page. Redacted BEFORE the truncation, so a cut credential cannot leave a usable head with no token to say anything was taken out. (6) *The outbox, a permanent second copy.* `auditor.record` redacted the line it appended and then pushed `record.action` RAW into `pending`; `sand_action_audit_logs` is pinned false, so `runFlush` takes the not-forwarding branch every five seconds and persists that array to `agents/audit-outbox.json` on the same persistent mount the agent can read. Measured read-only on the R750 2026-09-08: **394 `shellCommand` rows (219,890 bytes) on the demo box, 264 rows (800,907 bytes) on Jason's, 28 rows (67,391 bytes) on Richard's**; scanned against those boxes' own two secret stores, 0 hits today, so nothing has leaked -- but that is a property of the timing, not of the code, and turning the gate on would send the same strings off-box. `redactAuditAction` now runs at the push. Four new legs in `tests/audit-secret-redaction.test.mjs`, all four measured failing on the pre-fix tree and passing after: the persisted outbox, the forwarded batch, an **agent** turn's shell row (command and output), and a non-shell tool's serialized arguments **MEASURED ON THE R750 2026-09-09, after the swap.** All three boxes were moved to bundle `be06f0341a10` with `updateHostNow` (in place: every container's `StartedAt` is unchanged). Read on Jason's box through the same `getConversationOutline` the console calls: 5 conversations, 850 outline items, **186 `shellToolCall` rows of which 182 carry a populated `output`** -- the field that had no redactor -- and **0 occurrences of any of that box's 4 secret-store values**, with 1 redaction token present, so the redactor fired on real history rather than finding nothing to do. The before half is on the same disk: the TinyFish key (sha256 `9165ce2daa86`, len 44, the one this row is named for) is still in `agents/<id>/conversation-blobs.db` and in its quarantined copy, in the raw store the outline is derived from. The chip comes from providerHealth in cp/admin.mjs: with no live check on record it reads the proxy's request log for the month window and turns red on ANY failure. So a provider that failed three times yesterday and has answered every time since reads "not answering" for the rest of the month, while the key row beside it says "LAST ERROR none" because that column reads LiteLLM's always-empty /health/latest. Red only when the most recent five requests on the provider all failed, or a live check the operator asked for failed inside its TTL; otherwise green, with the month's failures as an amber count beside it in words. The five-deep window costs one addition inside spendReport's existing per-request loop in cp/proxy.mjs, and it is a keep-the-newest insert rather than an append, because that loop does not sort. The key row's LAST ERROR reads the same request log rather than /health/latest, and Check now with no key pasted says it needs the key. A unit test with three old failures and 217 later successes reads "answering". **MEASURED ON THE R750 2026-09-09 19:20 to 19:45 UTC, from this Mac (Darwin 25.6.0, node v22.23.1, headless Chromium through playwright-core 1.62.1) against api.titanium.bot, signed in with the operator token seeded into sessionStorage; Jason's account was not touched and no password was reset.** Before the ship, GET /v1/admin/providers from inside titanbot-cp read Alibaba Model Studio (token plan) **reachable false**, "3 of 284 request(s) ... failed inside this window". After the ship, with **no data change at all**, the same read answers **reachable true**, how **"5 of the last 5 request(s) went through"**, recent {count 5, failures 0}, month {requests **300**, failures **3**, lastFailureAt **2026-09-08T22:48:09.896Z**}. On screen the card reads a green **answering** chip with the amber line **"3 of 300 failed this month, last 2026-09-08 22:48 UTC"** beside it, and the panel's strip reads **NOT ANSWERING 0**. The key row qwen-1's LAST ERROR now carries the sweep's failure ({at 2026-09-08T22:48:09.896Z, why "litellm.AuthenticationError ... Incorrect API key provided"}) where LiteLLM's /health/latest answers an empty object for ever on this install; it draws as a red "an error" chip with the text and the time on its title. The row's earlier figures of 220 requests and 19:41 UTC were stale and are replaced by these. **ONE HOLE CLOSED 2026-09-09 22:00 to 22:45 UTC by a review of the shipped rule: the new red had no floor under it.** `recent.count > 0 && recent.failures === recent.count` bounded the window by count only, so a provider with fewer than five requests in the month went red on ONE stale failure and stayed red until the calendar rolled over -- the same stuck light this row was written to end, at low volume. Measured on the R750: **MiniMax's entire recent window is one request, from 2026-09-08T23:40:44Z**, and nothing else is ever run through it; had that request failed, the card would have read "not answering" off a twenty-hour-old sample of one for the rest of September, with no traffic coming to change it. Red now needs a sample deep enough AND fresh enough to be a claim about now: **at least three recent requests and the newest no more than two hours old**. Below either it falls through to the not-checked branch with the reason in words, never to green. Both cases are held by tests on this Mac (one failure alone reads "only 1 of them"; four failures a day old read "the most recent is from"), because neither can be made to happen on the R750 without breaking a provider somebody is using. **MEASURED ON THE R750 2026-09-09 22:41 UTC after the rebuild:** no chip changed colour -- Z.AI **answering** (5 of the last 5, month 328/0), MiniMax **answering** (1 of 1, month 1/0), Alibaba Model Studio (token plan) **answering** (5 of the last 5, month 329 requests, 3 failures, last 2026-09-08T22:48:09Z) | S |

| CONSOLE-4 (item A) · **landed and measured on grok-bot-local-vm** (2026-09-08) | Jason, 2026-09-08 20:03 and 20:04, four things he sees on console.titanium.bot. Three of them are item A's and each had a cause nobody had looked for. (1) **The mountains before the plate.** "console.titanium.bot initially shows the original background with the mountains, then reloads with whatever background the user chose." The chosen default was never wrong — `backgrounds.js` has answered the nebula since 5637e4b and his relay serves that byte for byte, so half of the ask had shipped two days earlier and he still saw mountains. The bug is load order: `index.html` chained `__bootMachineRoom` → `app.js` → `backgrounds.js`, so nothing set `data-bg` until fourteen serial gateway round trips had finished, and `styles.css` painted `assets/warmwind-landscape.svg` for the whole of that. Measured on `grok-bot-local-vm` over loopback on a warm box, sampling the shell's computed background every 25 ms: **1,247 ms** of mountains with nothing saved, **861 ms** with `habitat-3` saved. His console runs those round trips at 77–128 ms TTFB rather than loopback, so his flash is longer. (2) **The blank spots.** "One just says 'The Lab', the other one just says 'Habitat' ... they're just blank spots." `backgrounds.js` drew each series name as a `<p>` carrying an inline `flex-basis:100%`, and `.bg-grid` is `display:grid`, where `flex-basis` does nothing — so each heading took one tile's cell in the run of tiles. Measured before: **185 x 104** each, between two real tiles. It was a heading wearing a tile's box. (3) **The endless scroll.** "The chat for Titan just scrolls forever." Two causes, isolated by measurement, and both had to go or the complaint comes back reworded: `renderTranscript` computed `wasNearBottom` and threw it away, because the condition was `(!keepScroll \|\| wasNearBottom)` and **every `message:created` arrives with `keepScroll` false**; and `.transcript` carried `scroll-behavior: smooth`, so each of those writes was an animation across the full height. Measured on Atera Triage (190 rows, 15,908 px in a 668 px viewport), reader parked and left alone five seconds: dragged **9,830 px** (5,334 → 15,164) with the transcript in motion on **27.9 %** of 301 animation frames, never settling. Ruled out by measurement, so none of them was touched: scroll-to-top paging (already on `wheel`, fired zero times), image height shift (`scrollHeight` constant), the `foldRepeatedRows` rebuild. Under reduced motion, where `motion.css` already forces `auto`, the old code behaved — which is why the bug survived: the people who would have reported it were the ones the browser had already fixed it for | **Landed at `f73db6f`, relay-only — `git diff --name-only be06f03..f73db6f` names 21 files, all of them under `ui/machine-room`, `tests` or `docs`, and not one under `source/` or `deploy/`.** New `ui/machine-room/bg-boot.js` is a **classic blocking `<script src>` in `<head>`, before the first stylesheet link** — after them a classic script waits for the sheets to load, which is the cost being removed. It owns the default, the built-in list and the id-to-file rule; `backgrounds.js` reads them off `window.__mrBg` and keeps publishing `window.__machineRoomBackgrounds` unchanged, because two copies means a plate added to one of them flashes twice. The default is read off the entry marked `default: true` rather than written twice, and a test pins that the literal `titan-nebula` appears **exactly once** across the two files. `machineRoom.backgrounds.custom` is read only when the chosen id starts with `custom-`: that array holds data URLs up to 4,000,000 bytes and parsing it unconditionally in `<head>` would cost exactly what this saves. `apply()` sets `data-bg` for **every** id including `original` — it used to remove it and let the stylesheet's mountains stand in as the default, which is why a browser with nothing stored, or one whose storage throws, opened on a photograph; `styles.css` paints a flat brand Midnight floor instead (Cloud for the mist atmosphere, whose text is near-black) and `backgrounds.css` carries `html[data-bg="original"]` so picking Original still gives the mountains. Series headings are real heading rows (`grid-column: 1 / -1`, small caps, a hairline), the inline style gone. **The boot cover** is static markup as the first child of `<body>` so it paints with the first paint: the crew's Titan still (upgraded to a live `<titan-mascot>` once the element is defined and motion is allowed), "Setting up your console", a step line and a progress hint that is a hint rather than a percentage. It is opaque over the whole shell on purpose — `index.html` ships seed copy ("MSP Team", "3 members · ready", "2h 14m", "Atera Triage's desktop") that is fiction on a real box and was all on screen at 500 ms. It lifts on what a person can see (a `.worker-card`, then a `.message-row`; the roster and transcript are painted in one synchronous `renderAll`, so a card with an empty transcript is an empty conversation and resolves without waiting), on `data-demo` (where it says the console could not reach this box and gets out of the way rather than sitting friendly over the red DEMO DATA bar), or on an **8 s `setTimeout` armed at parse time and never chained to `hydrate()`'s promise** — that file has no `AbortController` and no per-call timeout anywhere and awaits every connector before asking for the first conversation, and BOOT-1 measured 46,318 ms to live with one cold stdio connector. Reduced motion removes the node outright; a 900 ms belt covers a `transitionend` that never fires. If the ceiling fires with no rows the transcript column says it is still opening the conversation rather than uncovering an empty page pretending to be done. **The scroll rule** is now: follow a reader already at the bottom, never move one who is not, and pin to the bottom once for a deliberate event — first paint, a conversation change (both `selectContext` and the adapter's `context:selected`, which covers the palette's jump and the landing after a create), and the reader's own send. `keepScroll` stays in the signature and is deliberately no longer consulted. `scroll-behavior` is off the container; `flashEntry` opts back into `{behavior:"smooth"}` at its own call site behind the reduced-motion query, which also made `renderTranscriptKeepingOffset`'s restore instant and removed the jump after *Show earlier messages*. **Measured after, same machine, same conversations:** mountains **0 ms** across 220 samples with and without a saved choice, `data-bg` already set at `readyState==="interactive"`; cover on screen at 50 ms and gone at **1,065 ms** / **1,143 ms**, ceiling never reached; parked reader **0 px** of drift and **0.0 %** of 301 frames; *Show earlier messages* holds the line (7,620 → 20,557) where it used to throw the reader to the foot (5,334 → 28,177); a reader at the foot is still followed. Series headings **776 x 23** spanning the grid, 18 tiles all carrying a picture, no tile-shaped blank. Gates: `tests/machine-room-boot.test.mjs` (34 checks; **31 of 34 fail on the pre-wave tree and all 34 pass after** — the three that always passed are the two behaviours that must not regress and one pure edge function), `npm test` **1580 PASS / 0 FAIL**, `node --test tests/` **1568 / 0**, `npm run source:typecheck` clean. Screenshots in the run's scratchpad (`shots/boot-50ms-*`, `shots/picker-section.png`, `shots/rail-tile.png`, `shots/attachment-row.png`, `shots/files-list.png`, and the same names under `shots-before/`). Contract and traps: `docs/CONSOLE.md` §1 | M |

| MEETING-1 · **decided, design final** (Jason, 2026-09-08 20:06 to 20:33) | Jason: "For the desktop app, I want it to be able to, like Wispr Flow, recognize when an app like Zoom, Google Meet, or Teams is running and automatically ask, 'Would you like to join the meeting?' It doesn't actually join; it just starts transcribing ... It would just listen to the microphone and the system audio." Then: "it should create a temporary room, and it should stream in as a meeting. That way Titan and I can chat. Any other agents I bring into that chat so they can be a part of the meeting." And the line that defines it: "They can't talk in the meeting. The meeting doesn't know they're there." The precedent is his own habit: Wispr Flow transcribing a meeting while he copied the transcript into Claude by hand to work alongside it | Owned, the desktop-app wave (after the thin native shells exist). Shape, as a ROOM: the desktop app notices a meeting window (Zoom, Google Meet in a browser tab, Microsoft Teams, FaceTime, Webex) and asks once, "Titan can take notes on this meeting. Start?"; on yes it creates a temporary room named for the meeting and the date with Titan in it, captures the microphone and the system audio (macOS ScreenCaptureKit audio, Screen Recording permission once; Windows WASAPI loopback), streams both to a transcriber (Deepgram streaming, a key Jason holds, or a local Whisper for privacy), and posts the transcript into the room as a speaker named Meeting, one message per speaker turn appended live until that person stops talking, speaker-labelled where the transcriber can; Jason types in the room, Titan and any agent he brings in read the meeting as it happens and answer him in the room; nothing an agent says goes into the call, no audio is ever played, no video is recorded, and the app says on screen that it is listening; when the window closes the room is archived with the transcript saved as a file (CONSOLE-4's viewer opens it) and a summary offered **Design notes, 2026-09-08 20:14 (Jason's questions, my answers, to confirm):** (1) Chunking: never word by word into the room. Deepgram streaming gives interim text, an utterance end and a speaker label; the room gets one message per finished speaker turn, consecutive turns by the same speaker within about ten seconds merged into one paragraph; the in-progress words show as a live caption line at the foot of the room, not as a message, so nothing is ever edited after it is posted and Titan never answers half a sentence. (2) Speakers: the operator's microphone and the system audio are two streams, so the operator is known for certain ("You") and everyone else is Speaker 1, 2, 3 from diarization; the operator renames a speaker once in the room ("Speaker 2 is Richard") and Titan may propose a name he infers from the audio ("Hi Richard") and ask; names stick for the meeting. (3) The room brief: a pinned first message the agents read: the meeting's name, time and app, the calendar event's title and attendees when the Google connector has them, the one-way rule, the roles (Titan: the operator's VA in the meeting, note-taker, answers what the operator types, decides whom to invite; invited agents: observers unless addressed by name), and what is wanted at the end. (4) Titan invites: through the room membership commands he already has, saying in the room who he brought and why, with the observer rule in his words. (5) During: Titan keeps a running card (decisions so far, action items with owners, open questions) that updates as the meeting goes; agents with tools research on the fly (files, mail, browser, TinyFish) and answer in the room only. (6) After: the whole transcript is saved as a Markdown file with meeting timestamps and speakers (the room's own history keeps it too), the room is archived, Titan produces the summary and the action items, offers to draft the follow-up email from the operator's address (MAIL-1), to file the action items as routines or reminders, and to attach the notes to the calendar event. (7) Privacy: audio is never stored, only text; a visible listening indicator; a local Whisper option for a customer who will not send audio out; retention is the room's. (8) Cost: Deepgram streaming is about $0.0077 a minute; it rides the proxy's pass-through per tenant like TinyFish so it is metered on the client row. **MEETING-1 addendum to apply to the row once the lock is free (Jason, 20:**31, "Deepgram first, agreed"): a meeting room is its own kind of room, not a plain room with a feed. Its right rail is the meeting: an Attendees card that fills as speakers are named (You first, then each Speaker N as it is renamed, with the calendar's attendee list offered as the names to pick from), the running card (decisions, action items with owners, open questions, updating live), and a Research card where every link, file and answer an agent brought during the meeting collects with who brought it and when; the caption line for the words in flight at the foot of the transcript; the listening indicator and the Stop control in the rail's header. Deepgram streaming with diarization first; a local Whisper option later. Status: decided, design final. **MEETING-1, 20:**32: archived, never deleted. A meeting room is archived when the meeting ends and stays in a Meetings section of the roster, searchable, with its transcript file, its running card and its research card as they were; the operator can reopen any past meeting and ask Titan about it ("what did we promise Bay Roofing in March"), and Titan can read past meeting rooms when a new meeting with the same attendees starts. Jason: "This would be an incredibly powerful tool." **MEETING-1, 20:**33, the two Jasons (Jason: "It's an in-meeting Jason, not an out-of-meeting Jason"): the operator appears in a meeting room as TWO distinct speakers. What the microphone hears is posted as "Jason (in the meeting)" inside the transcript feed, drawn like the other meeting speakers (the feed's own style, a meeting glyph, no reply affordance), and the agents are told in the brief that those words are addressed to the people on the call, not to them: they read, they do not answer. What the operator TYPES in the room is "Jason", the ordinary room message, addressed to the agents, and that is what they answer. The brief states the rule in one sentence and the two are visibly different in the room so the person never confuses them either. A hotword or push-to-talk that turns a spoken sentence into a typed one is a later option, off by default. | L |
| VOICE-1 · **design, 2026-09-09 06:41, Jason** | Voice as a first-class way to work with Titan, Jarvis style: you talk to the computer and the head of the team answers. Sources Jason named: `wombatoperator/omarchy-voice` (OpenAI Realtime over a websocket, speech becomes tool calls, a local policy gate, mic held shut while it speaks plus 350 ms, a typed `say` twin, announcements only for finished jobs, listening is a toggle and never always-on) and AmpCortex `apps/api/src/lib/voice-bridge.ts` (xAI realtime at `wss://api.x.ai/v1/realtime`, the same event shape as OpenAI: `session.update`, `server_vad`, `response.function_call_arguments.done`, `function_call_output`; dedupe on call_id because xAI emits the call twice). Since both providers speak the same wire, one bridge serves both and xAI is the default because it is cheaper | **Design.** (1) The voice is a mouth and ears, not a second brain: the realtime model gets one tool, `titan(message)`, which sends the utterance into Titan's conversation through the gateway and returns his reply, so memory, persona, team and the approval flows stay Titan's and the thread stays one thread (voice turns land in the transcript tagged as spoken). The realtime model acknowledges while Titan works and speaks the reply as it streams. (2) Held actions are spoken back ("Send it?") and a yes closes them through the existing approval path, never a second gate. (3) The relay holds the socket to the provider with the workspace's key from the Providers panel, meters minutes per tenant into the ledger, xAI default and OpenAI selectable. (4) Two doors: a talk button and orb in the console (browser audio over a websocket to the relay) and a hotkey in the desktop app sharing MEETING-1's capture. Toggle only, no wake word, mic shut while Titan speaks. (5) Reuse: AmpCortex's `driveSession` is already TypeScript and becomes the relay bridge; omarchy-voice's session gating, announcement queue and rate-limit wait are ported; its capability manifest is not needed because Titan already has his tools. Wave V, second batch with D and E | M |
| SECRET-ROTATE-1 · **new, needs Jason** (2026-09-08) | Two secrets were exposed into an agent transcript during this wave's measurement, by the measuring script and not by the product. **`ZAI_API_KEY` (49 characters, sha256 `06c56620cbac`)**: LiteLLM's 422 validation error echoes the request body, and a probe script printed that body whole while working out why the credential PATCH was failing. The product itself does not leak it — `cp/proxy.mjs` only ever takes STRING candidates out of an error body and a 422's `detail` is an array of objects, so the control plane printed `the proxy answered 422` with nothing after it, and the panel would have shown the same. **demo's virtual key** (`titanbot-demo`): a script read `/data/titanbot/demo/profile/model-proxy.json` whole to check the stored plan rows. Both should be rotated: `node cp/cli.mjs proxy rotate demo` for the tenant key, and the Z.AI key through the Providers panel's roll once a new one is minted at Z.AI — which is now a 0.26 s operation with no restart and no failed request, which is the point of the wave. | S |
