| HANDBACK-1 · **landed and measured on the R750** (2026-09-08) | Jason, 2026-09-08 12:46, five screenshots of the original product's desktop app: "I want this exact workflow." What the console actually did with a hand-off: `messagesOf` threw all three box fields away, so the instruction the host stamped `boxRequestId` / `boxInstruction` on landed as a plain agent bubble and the only control anywhere was a button in the footer of a centred dialog (GW-10). Four defects under that, each measured on `grok-bot-local-vm` during the design pass. (1) **A skip was indistinguishable from a done:** `handBackForeverBox {trigger:"cancel"}` wrote the entry resolution `cancelled` AND resumed the agent with the handed-back prompt, so the agent carried on as if the sign-in had happened. There was no skip command at all. (2) **The pending record did not survive a restart:** `BoxHandoffService.pending` was a bare `Map` with no writer, so a host restart lost the card while `awaitingUserResponse` (persisted in the agent's own db) kept the roster pill up, and the two disagreed. (3) **The status carried a picture:** `decorateForeverBoxStatus` forwarded the whole `PendingHandoff` including `snapshotDataUrl`; `getForeverBoxStatus` went **284 B to 10,013 B** while pending **on a blank screen**, and this box's real screenshots are **47 to 52 KB** (about 70 KB base64), pulled by `loadContext` on every 15 s heartbeat and every 900 ms-debounced stream tick. It was stale as well: the host still said `about:blank` while a live read of the same seat showed the site the agent had navigated to. (4) **The hand-back call blocked on the revived turn:** `sand-host.ts` awaited `resumeAfterBoxHandoff` inside the handler, measured on the same box at **663 ms** on one run and **9,866 ms with the socket closed at 58,917 ms** on another | The hand-off is one durable transcript entry (`boxRequestId` + `boxInstruction`, later `boxResolution`) plus a live pending record, and the card is a function of BOTH, never of one: `pending` (pill `Action needed`), `done` (`Done`), `skipped` (`Skipped`), and `closed` (`No longer waiting`) for an entry with no live match and no resolution this path wrote, which is real (`box-request-entries.ts` silently resolves a prior entry as `dismissed` when a second request lands) and is the only thing standing between a person and a card that reads Done on a step nobody did. **One vocabulary:** resolutions written from now on are `handed_back` and `dismissed`, the resume prompt is keyed off the RESOLUTION rather than a raw trigger string, and `completed` / `cancelled` are read-side aliases so nothing on Richard's box or the demo tenant is rewritten. **`skipBoxHandoff {id}`** is a new command; on a host that predates it every Skip control is simply not drawn, because the tempting fallback (`handBackForeverBox {trigger:"dismissed"}`) reaches the declined prompt but stamps the entry `completed`. **The pending record is a 0600 sidecar** the service owns, temp-file-plus-rename, read synchronously in the constructor (`decorateForeverBoxStatus` calls `pendingHandoff()` from a non-async method, so async there ripples into every status read). **The status carries no image:** `{requestId, instruction, startedAt, snapshotAt?}`; `captureSnapshot` and its telemetry are untouched, they just stopped riding the wire. **The picture is the box's own screen through the relay** (`/vnc/<display>/`, same origin, untainted canvas), one hidden client per open conversation ticked every 3 s pushing a data URL into a plain `<img>`, never a client inside the transcript card (`transcriptMarkup` rebuilds the whole list and noVNC re-runs its handshake when its element is replaced). **The resume is fire-and-forget** after the entry is stamped and the status emitted. Three surfaces, all on the same pending hand-off: the transcript card (Take over / I'm done / Skip, then one Open computer), the rail's amber `Needs your attention` card above the screen tile captioned `<name>'s screen`, and the takeover banner across the full-window desktop view, which inherited GW-10's `#hand-back` and `#hand-back-note` ids so the dashboard gate still points at the real control. The collapse arrow closes without deciding. Contract and traps: `docs/HANDOFF.md`. Gate: `scripts/verify-handoff.mjs --host \| --console \| --restart`, one at a time inside the 300 s ceiling, each creating and deleting its own scratch agent and asking for the hand-off BY INTENT (asked for `request_box_help` by name the model has answered that it does not have it while the host's own toolset line listed it), asserting only on `getForeverBoxStatus.handoff`, and ending on an ordinary prompt that must get a reply because an interrupt-and-resume has left a conversation silent once. **Measured on `grok-bot-local-vm` (this Mac), design pass:** 1,350 ms to a painted framebuffer on the agent's own seat; 5 ms and about 7 KB per 390x244 webp at q0.6; 5.8 % of one core for a live client; a `display:none` client keeps painting and a second viewer does not kick the first; `ensureForeverBox` 16,277 ms cold, which is why nothing calls it for a picture. **Measured on the R750, 2026-09-08, host `ee8c10006d40`.** The wire half ran inside the demo tenant box `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau` with a real turn: prompt to a pending hand-off **7.6 s** (**15.1 s** on the second pass); the whole box status **293 B** with the hand-off object **144 B**, keys `requestId,instruction,startedAt` and no image; the entry unresolved while the person held the computer; `handBackForeverBox` answered in **16 ms** and the entry read `handed_back` **21 ms** later, which is the resume no longer blocking the command; the agent's next message **17.6 s** after that; `skipBoxHandoff` answered in **6 ms**, stamped `dismissed`, the host stopped reporting the hand-off, and the agent's next message landed **10.1 s** later saying it could not go on until the sign-in was done; an ordinary prompt after both decisions still got a reply in **27.6 s**, so nothing wedged. The browser half ran in real Chrome from Jason's Mac against `console.titanium.bot`: prompt to the card on screen **10.3 s**, pill `Action needed`, three buttons; **first thumbnail frame 1.5 s, 5,395 characters of webp data URL (about 4 KB)** and it is the agent's own screen, the sign-in page it had open; the rail's amber card up; a full reload mid-hand-off drew the same pending card; Take over opened the view full window (`data-takeover="1"`, banner **1598x55**) with the instruction and both buttons hit-testable; `I'm done, continue` closed the view and the card read **Done in 1.1 s** with one `Open computer` button, the rail card gone; the agent's next message appeared **22.0 s** later with no reload, and its own words were that it had checked the desktop first. No page errors. Screenshots of all five states in the run's scratchpad (`shots/r750-01..05`). **On grok-bot-local-vm the same day:** `verify-handoff --host` 14 PASS / 0 FAIL, `--console` 31 PASS / 0 FAIL, `--restart` 4 PASS / 0 FAIL, `verify-dashboard` 280 PASS with its GW-10 legs green. **One thing the R750 pass taught:** the card first demanded a per-agent forever-box seat before it would draw a picture, and an agent that browses for 95 s on this image still reports `state absent` with no `vncUrl`, so the ordinary hand-off showed no picture at all. Falling back to the shared seat on display `:1` fixed the blank card and introduced HANDBACK-2 below, which is the honest end of this row: **the sentence "it is the agent's own screen, the sign-in page it had open" above was not true on that run** | L |
| HANDBACK-2 · **landed** (2026-09-08) | Two blockers a review found in the shipped HANDBACK-1, each measured live in real Chrome on `console.titanium.bot`. (1) **The picture was of the wrong screen.** The card's reader and the rail tile were mounted on `/vnc/1/` while the desktop view `Take over` opened was `/vnc/5/`, and `/home/box/.sand-window-assignments.json` inside `titanbot-box-p927bfqm83ioloibamlvyd7g` held `:5` for that agent. `getForeverBoxStatus` answered `state absent` with `vncUrl` null — the ordinary state of an agent whose screen nobody has opened — so the console fell back to the shared seat and captioned another agent's wallpaper `<name>'s screen`. A person decided on that picture; the agent's own next line said the browser was on a blank page. (2) **Every successful Skip told the person their software was too old**, and then hid Skip from the card, the rail and the banner for the rest of the session. `skipBoxHandoff` returned `void`, which on the wire is http 200 with the four bytes `null` — byte for byte what the gateway answers for a command it has never heard of — and the console inferred support from that answer. Three smaller ones with them: a resolved card said `Bringing the screen up` for ever in any browser that had not captured a frame while the step was pending and an idle agent's rail tile said `Connecting` permanently; the takeover view was edge to edge, so the dimmed app the spec, the doc and the stylesheet's own comment all promise was never visible; and the banner prefixed the instruction with the agent's name, so one step read three ways in three places | **The host answers the question instead of the console guessing it.** `getForeverBoxStatus` carries `boxSeat`, read out of the box's own assignment map with no allocation: a number is the agent's own seat, `null` is no seat of its own and the caption then says `The shared screen — every agent on this box sees it`, and an absent field means this host cannot say, so no picture is drawn at all and the plate says so. The desktop view's own mount feeds back the display it really painted, so a seat `Take over` allocates is what the picture reads from at once. `skipBoxHandoff` and `handBackForeverBox` answer `{ok:true}`, and the console reads `tryCall`'s own record of unknown commands rather than the answer. The card's plate is a function of the state like the pill is, the tile says `Connecting` only while a reader is running, the takeover view is inset 32 px so the dim reads around it, and the banner carries the instruction verbatim. **Gates that would have caught them, added:** after a Skip the console gate fails on a `/too old/i` toast and on Skip having gone; it reads the thumbnail reader's src and the desktop view's src and fails unless the display numbers match; and it fails a resolved card still promising a picture. The host gate asserts `boxSeat` is on the wire and that a skip answers something a success can be told by. **Measured on grok-bot-local-vm 2026-09-08:** `getForeverBoxStatus` for an agent with assignment `:3` and `vncUrl` null answered `boxSeat: 3`, for an agent with none `boxSeat: null`; `skipBoxHandoff` answered `{"ok":true}`; `verify-handoff --host` 17 PASS / 0 FAIL and `--console` 33 PASS / 0 FAIL with the reader, the desktop view and the box's assignment file all on display `:4`; a Skip in real Chrome flipped the card to Skipped in 1.4 s with the toast "Skipped — the agent has been told the step was not done" and Skip still supported afterwards; the takeover view 1536x936 inset 32 px on every side in a 1600x1000 window; `verify-dashboard`'s GW-10 legs green with the note now the instruction alone. **Measured on the R750, bundle `37050858e4ec` on all three boxes:** one real turn inside the demo box `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau` reached a pending hand-off in 10.2 s with the status 296 B carrying `state absent`, `vncUrl null` and `boxSeat 4` — the exact case that used to draw display `:1` — `skipBoxHandoff` answered `{"ok":true}` in 10 ms, the entry read `dismissed` and the agent said it had skipped it. **Not re-measured: the browser half on the R750.** Signing in at console.titanium.bot needs `DEMO_PASSWORD` from `cp.env`, which this wave may not read; what is established there is the wire above plus the shipped bytes (`sha256` of `ui/machine-room/app.js` and `styles.css` on the server equal the shipped worktree's). Owner: whoever holds the console password; next action: `scripts/verify-handoff.mjs --console` against console.titanium.bot | M |
| CONSOLE-4-TILE · **landed and measured on the merged console, grok-bot-local-vm** (2026-09-08) | Jason, 2026-09-08 20:03: "The Titan screen at the top right says 'Click to open,' but there's a broken image there. I think that's supposed to be a screenshot of what's on the browser at that moment." **Two causes under the one glyph, both measured before anything was written.** (1) `renderScreenTile` always emitted the `<img>` and marked it `hidden`, while `styles.css` says `.rail-screen-button img { display: block }` — one class and one type, against the UA sheet's one-attribute `[hidden] { display: none }`. The display rule wins on specificity, the element goes on being laid out, and an `<img>` being laid out with no `src` is precisely where Chrome paints its own broken-image glyph and the alt text. Measured on his console: `hidden` true, computed `display` block, no `src` attribute, `naturalWidth` 0, a **231x75** box. Reproduced in real Chrome on `grok-bot-local-vm` 2026-09-08 at **231x86**, alt text "Unread probe 1788914379301 renamed's screen", screenshot `console4-tile/tile-before-1788919355891.png`. The same stylesheet already carries this exact belt one screen up for `.handoff-thumb[hidden]` and `.handoff-island[hidden]`. (2) Behind the glyph there was nothing to show: `HANDBACK-1`'s reader only ever mounts while a hand-off is **pending**, so an idle agent — which is what Titan is nearly all day — had no frame in memory, none in storage and no client running to make one | **Two halves, and they ship in two commits by design.** `screen-tile.css` carries the belt (`.rail-screen-button img[hidden]`, `img:not([src])`, `img[src=""]` → `display: none`) so no future path can re-open the trap whatever `app.js` emits; `ui/machine-room/screen-tile.js` is the missing reader. It mounts ONE hidden `view_only` noVNC client for the open conversation, built exactly the way `boxHandoffEnsureThumb` builds one, and keeps five rules: one reader at a time (it stands down for `HANDBACK-1`'s through `window.__machineRoomHandoff.screen().readerDisplay` rather than opening a second websocket on the same seat); **a still by default**, refreshing about every 5 s only while the record's status is `working`, because the alternative is a standing screen-share of the operator's own browsing — the seat read on 2026-09-08 had Gmail, a GitHub account and a YouTube channel open on it; never torn down on a render that merely carried no display (the roster and the box status are two reads and a record is briefly seatless between them; tearing down there took the first frame from about 1.4 s to 33 s); a hidden tab drops it to zero while an open desktop dialog only pauses it; and **a frame that cannot be told from blank is refused** — the first sample on Titan's seat was **1,043 characters of solid white** and the next **7,591 of the real desktop**, so the guard needs both at least 2,048 characters AND a luminance spread of at least 10/255 read off the thumbnail's own pixels, since a genuinely dark screen compresses small too. Idle stills live under `mr-screen-tile-frame:<agentId>` with their own index capped at three, so they cannot be chosen for eviction by `HANDBACK-1`'s newest-eight walk of ITS index; about 5.7 KB each, the two stores near 63 KB together. The plate keeps `HANDBACK-2`'s exact three wordings, drawn on the Ti mark inline as a data-URI SVG over a faint cyan halo on Midnight rather than a bare dark box. **The gate: `scripts/verify-console-polish.mjs --tile`, 21 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP on grok-bot-local-vm 2026-09-08** — the belt (computed `display: none`, box 0x0, against block in a 231x86 box before it), zero laid-out images with nothing to draw across all three seat branches, six renders mounting exactly one reader, one reader surviving a seatless render and dropping to zero on a hidden tab, **a real 2,479-character webp of the box's own desktop on `:3` in 5.2 s with the plate holding until it landed** and `naturalWidth` 390 in a 231x145 tile (`console4-tile/tile-picture-1788919361594.png`), the blank-frame guard refusing the 1,043-character sample and taking the 7,591-character one, the reader costing no measurable frames (181 frames over 3 s with and without it, 0.0 % over 20 ms on this Mac), the tile hit-testable at 233x146 and clicking it opening the desktop view. Unit: `tests/machine-room-screen-tile.test.mjs`, 32 tests. Doc: `docs/CONSOLE.md` §3. **Not yet true of the shipped page:** `index.html` carries no `<script src="screen-tile.js">` and `renderScreenTile` still emits the src-less `<img>`; both land with CONSOLE-4-A, and until then the gate measures the shipped module injected into the real page and says so in its own output | M |
| CONSOLE-4-SEAT-ARG · **landed as a gate assertion** (2026-09-08) | `getForeverBoxStatus` adds `boxSeat` only when `status.agentId` is a non-empty string, so the `{ agentId }` argument form silently answers a stub with **no `boxSeat` field at all** — and the console's own contract (`HANDBACK-2`) reads an absent field as "this host cannot say which screen this agent is on". It also asserts the default is `titan-nebula`, which it already is: measured 2026-09-08, `DEFAULT_CHOICE="titan-nebula"` across 18 plates, so the mountains Jason still sees are the boot-order flash (CONSOLE-4-A), not the default | S |

| BOX-6 · **the door is closed; damage already done is NOT undone by it — see BOX-6b** (2026-09-09) | *Header corrected 2026-09-09 as BOX-6b asked: "proved gone" was read as "no agent is in this state any more", and one customer-facing agent was, for two days after this row was written. The store's copy is stale by construction: `box-store-sync` cannot snapshot a busy database (`snapshot failed; uncaptured` every cycle), so the restored pages no longer match the live WAL and every turn after that failed with `database disk image is malformed`. The only skip that existed, `localFileMatches`, compares sha256, which a live database never matches. **It was armed and running, not dormant:** `docker logs` on Jason's box 2026-09-08 carried `[box-copy-in] result outcome=hydrated store_entries=1607 files=1607 bytes=732641817` — 732 MB written over a customer's data at the last start | **Landed.** Both databases are out of `COPY_IN_CRITICAL_BASENAMES` entirely, and `isAgentDatabaseRelPath` (the two names plus their `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal` sidecars) keeps them out of every other phase whenever a live regular file is already on the persistent mount — existence, not mtime, because a store copy that carries a newer stamp is still a snapshot of a busy file. A box with no sand-data yet still hydrates. The copy-in's completeness test counts CANDIDATES rather than manifest entries, without which a skipped database reads as a partial hydrate and retries eight times (caught by the gate below on its first run: 1639/1659, 20 left alone, 0 failures, `outcome=failed`). Startup recovery now tries `REINDEX` before quarantining anything and `sqlite3 .recover` before the row-by-row salvage, which is the difference between 5 rows and 2,940 on a file with one bad btree page. **Measured on grok-bot-local-vm by a real container recreate** (`scripts/verify-box-copy-in.mjs`, which seeds the store with a copy that differs from the live file, recreates, and reads back): before the fix, both databases came back holding the store's stale copy; after, both hold what the live box wrote, the ordinary file beside them was restored (the control leg, without which a copy-in that did nothing would pass), the host logged `copy-in left 20 live agent database(s) as they are`, and `outcome=hydrated store_entries=1668 files=1648`. `tests/box-copy-in-agent-db.test.mjs` pins the basenames, because a unit test over the restore functions cannot see a name being added back **Two gaps in the guard, closed 2026-09-09 before either could bite.** (1) *The third database.* `grep -rhoE '"[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+\.db"' source/host/` returns exactly three names and `AGENT_DATABASE_BASENAMES` held two. `search-index.db` lives at `join(getSandRootDir(), SEARCH_INDEX_FILENAME)` -- `/home/box/sand-data/search-index.db`, the same bind-mounted volume as the other two -- and `deploy/box-defaults/gates.json` sets `sand_global_search: true`, so the day a box builds that index a recreate reproduces this row's corruption for it exactly. It is not on disk on the demo box today, which is what makes this latent rather than live. Added, and the `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal` handling in `isAgentDatabaseRelPath` covers its sidecars for free. (2) *The symlink phase.* The skip guarded only the `fileEntries` loop; the symlink loop below it restored unconditionally, so a manifest symlink entry named `store.db` would unlink a live database and put a link in its place -- the same corruption through a different door. Same predicate now applies there. `scripts/verify-box-copy-in.mjs` asserts all three names survive a recreate rather than the two it named | M |

| BOX-6b · **landed, shipped and measured on the R750; the customer-facing agent answers again; the row's own premise was wrong and the correction is the finding** (2026-09-09) | **The failure, as the customer met it.** Measured read-only on the demo tenant's box `titanbot-box-atonqjq7zx593jsacaccpfau`, 2026-09-09: agent `c63fdce4-4fc0-4ea7-8a1b-93657df2c6c5` had failed EVERY turn since 2026-09-07 23:00:44Z, **18 times in one host log**, always `[sand][turn] agent run failed for c63fdce4-… TranscriptJournalCorruptionError: transcript checkpoint must recover before preparing`, and every one of them reached the person as "could not finish that one. Ask again, or send the details to the developers." Asking again fails identically for ever. **The premise of this row as written was wrong, and correcting it changed the whole build.** Nothing was corrupt. `PRAGMA integrity_check` on both `store.db` and `conversation-blobs.db` = `ok`; no `.corrupt-*` anywhere under sand-data; the transcript directory held exactly ONE file, a 2-byte `<id>.journal-mode` marker written 2026-09-07 23:00:44Z, with no `.jsonl`, no pending log and no cursor. **Counts, R750, 2026-09-09:** `transcript_entries` 115, `kv` 5, `blobs` 0; `conversation-blobs.db` blobs 5,002 across 157,093,888 B; the agent directory 221 MB against 1.9 T free. The damage was a durable ROUTE MARKER plus a recovery the turn path never calls: `RoutedTranscriptMirror.selectRoute` pins a conversation to the journal for ever once the marker exists, `FileTranscriptMirror.prepareCheckpoint` throws whenever its in-memory maps have no entry, and those maps are filled only by `recover()` / `commitCheckpoint()` / `skipCheckpoint()` — of which the only reachable `recover()` sits behind `transcriptPersistenceEnabled === false`, which production hardcodes true. So the first prepare of every host process threw and every turn failed after it. That also explains the missing `.jsonl`: `initialize()`, which builds it from the checkpoint and the blob store, runs only inside `recover()` and had never once executed for this agent. **Blast radius one agent:** that box carries 1 marker across 8 transcript directories (the other 7 hold plain `.jsonl` files); Jason's box `p927bfqm83ioloibamlvyd7g` and Richard's `wepegxhh3fpvr83bubvz5xm5` carry none and have zero corruption lines in their host logs. Richard's box needed nothing and was never touched. | **Landed, in two halves.** *The host:* self-recovery on the first failed prepare (quarantine by rename, never delete; rebuild from the journal and the blob store; count before and after; one log line; retry the prepare once), the `repairAgentTranscript` verb answering `{before, after, quarantined, outcome}` with an audit row, and the needs-repair state on the roster record when recovery refuses. The sqlite `REINDEX` / `.recover` / quarantine machinery this row's original next-action asked for **already existed** (`source/host/storage/sqlite-recovery.ts`, wired into `conversation-blob-db.ts`) and is the right tool for the OTHER damage shape; building it again would have shipped sqlite repair for a healthy file and left the demo Titan refusing every turn. *The console:* one shared predicate in `gateway-adapter.js` drives both surfaces, so the conversation line and the card cannot drift — the failed turn now says **"This agent's conversation store needs repair. No repair has ever returned it. What a real host answered is in the box legs below. — the pill on the roster and not on the ordinary failure beside it, the need-you count uninflated, the card leading with the sentence, the Repair control hit-tested at 65x34 with nothing on top of it, one `repairAgentTranscript` call on a real mouse click, the stub's `115` rendered as "Repaired, 115 entries kept.", the control and the pill gone afterwards, and the next turn completing with its answer on the page. **Box leg on grok-bot-local-vm: 12 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP** — a scratch agent held a turn (0 → 2 entries), was damaged with the two-byte marker exactly the way the demo Titan was, and the turn after it completed (2 → 4) with the entries it already had kept. **That leg proved less than it claimed, corrected in the review round:** the route a conversation takes is decided once and cached for the life of the host process, so a marker written AFTER the agent's first turn changed nothing and the turn would have completed anyway. The judge is the outcome word alone now (`already-healthy` / `recovered` / `reset` / `refused`), it lives in one place, and both shapes are pinned. `tests/machine-room-transcript-repair.test.mjs` 31 cases, every block sliced out of the shipped files rather than copied; `npm test` 1,946 PASS / 0 FAIL; typecheck clean. **Two things measured and NOT fixed here, owned rather than noted.** (1) *The adapter's line into the conversation is written and never painted.* Recorded frame by frame in the browser: `reloadTrays` pushes it and `loadContext` replaces that record's messages wholesale in the same cycle with no paint between, for the active context and for one you walk over to. The useful half, `transcript checkpoint must recover before preparing`, survives and is what the gate asserts. **Owner: whoever next takes FEEDBACK-1's custody surface. Backed up first, inside the box, on the same volume: `/home/box/sand-data/agent-backups/c63fdce4-4fc0-4ea7-8a1b-93657df2c6c5-2026-09-09T20-41-53Z`, **221 MB**, 118 entries and 5,023 blobs in the copy. (Under `agent-backups/`, not under `agents/`: a directory there is scanned as an agent and a copy with a store in it draws a second roster row.) **The repair, through the product — and what actually unwedged the agent, corrected 2026-09-09 in the review round.** `repairAgentTranscript` — the identical call the console's Repair button makes — answered `{"before":0,"after":0,"quarantined":[],"outcome":"already-healthy","reason":"this conversation store had nothing to repair"}` and wrote one `transcript_repair` row into that agent's ledger at 20:46:27.956Z. **That verb contributed nothing to the fix and this row used to imply it did.** Re-verified read-only on the R750 today: that ledger holds exactly ONE `transcript_repair` row, `already-healthy`, before 0, after 0, quarantined `[]`; the host log line beside it is `nothing to repair in the conversation store for c63fdce4-…: 0 entries before, 0 after`. The line that follows it, `repaired the conversation store for c63fdce4-…: 0 entries before, 8 after`, is **the message's own in-turn recovery in the swapped bundle** — the host half, not the button. So the customer-facing agent is genuinely back, and it was self-recovery that brought it back. The on-demand path therefore had NO measurement against a really damaged agent until the review round below. **The message, 20:46:27Z.** The host log gained exactly one line, `[sand][transcript] repaired the conversation store for c63fdce4-…: 0 entries before, 8 after, nothing set aside`, and no new failure. **After:** the conversation file exists — 4,217 bytes, **11 entries** — the store **118 → 120**, blobs **5,023 → 5,042**, and `TranscriptJournalCorruptionError` still **19**, so not one more since the bundle landed. Asked *"Titan, are you back? Answer in one short sentence"* it answered **"Yes, I'm here."** six seconds later and then worked through the backlog two days of failed turns had left it. **The verb also answers at the console's own address:** `POST https://console.titanium.bot/api/repairAgentTranscript` for the operator's own Titan returned `{"before":750,"after":750,"quarantined":[],"outcome":"already-healthy"}`, and the production console loads the BOX-6b console code in real Chrome (`window.__transcriptRepair` live, 6 roster cards, 0 needs-repair pills because nothing on that box needs one, 0 page errors). **MEASURED AT INTEGRATION on this Mac, merged tree `39f588d`:** `npm test` 2,088 tests with one failure that is not this wave's (`tests/cp-marketplace-admin.test.mjs`, red at the tip `d9cf40f` without this wave applied — see MARKET-VERIFY-1); `source:typecheck` clean; `build-host.mjs` `validated-clean-source clean=true`. On grok-bot-local-vm: `verify-transcript-repair` console **26 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP** and `--box` **12 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP**; `verify-dashboard` **281 PASS / 3 FAIL**, the three being DASH-4's known pre-existing rows. **NOT run: the demo tenant's own console in a browser.** That login needs `DEMO_PASSWORD` out of `/home/sem/titanbot/cp.env`, which this wave is forbidden to read; the operator bearer opens the operator's tenant only. The repair was therefore made with the verb, which is the door the button calls, at the box gateway and again at `console.titanium.bot`. Screenshots of the console states are in the scratchpad at `box6b-shots/`. **REVIEW ROUND, 2026-09-09 — six findings, one a blocker; all fixed and re-measured on this Mac (M3) and on grok-bot-local-vm.** (1) *Pressing Repair threw away a live turn.* `repairTranscriptFiles` renamed ANY `<id>.journal-pending.json` to `.corrupt-<stamp>` without reading it, so a press while a turn held a write-ahead copy dropped the turn that copy was carrying and the console called it kept. Measured against the shipped source: control run, nobody presses Repair, the next recover replays and the conversation file is 3 lines; press Repair first and it is 2, with the verb answering `before 2, after 2, recovered`. The verb now PARSES that file (`parsePendingCheckpoint`) and sets aside only one that cannot be parsed; a valid one is left where it is. Pinned both ways, and the new test fails on the old code. (2) *The verb raced the turn it was meant to rescue.* It moved journal files outside the mirror's per-conversation write lane, so a press between `prepareCheckpoint` and `commitCheckpoint` made the commit throw `prepared transcript WAL is missing at commit` — the button causing the state it exists to clear. `runInTranscriptWriteLane` is exported from `transcript-mirror.ts` (the same module-level lane `serialize` uses, which now delegates to it) and the verb runs inside it. (3) *A latch-only clear answered `recovered`, and the panel said "Repaired, 0 entries kept."* — a false success on the one case the state exists for, and it looped: the next message re-latched and nothing counted the attempts. There is a fourth outcome now, `cleared`, deliberately outside the adapter's success words, and the panel says *Cleared the stuck state. Send this agent one message: if it answers, it is back. If it fails the same way, this store needs a person, and the reason it gave was: …*. The marker carries the clear count and the original reason, a SECOND press is refused with that reason instead of clearing again, and a recovery that actually works forgets the count. (4) *The needs-repair half had never run on a host, and the leg meant to prove it could not.* Its damage (a pending log matching nothing) is set aside and recovered from on the second attempt, so it always reported SKIP; `--refuse` was also opt-in and was not in the 12-PASS run. `--refuse` is its own mode now and the damage is one no retry can pass — a DIRECTORY where the conversation file belongs, so the rebuild ends in a rename onto a directory (chmod means nothing: the host runs as root inside the box). **MEASURED on grok-bot-local-vm, 2026-09-09: `--refuse` 11 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP** — the marker written with its reason (`EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory, read`, `clears: 0`), `listAgents` carrying `transcriptNeedsRepair`, the host logging `could not repair the conversation store for e37bd7ea-…` with no class names in it, the verb answering `{"before":0,"after":0,"quarantined":[],"outcome":"cleared"}`, and the message after it completing (2 → 4 entries) with the conversation file rebuilt to 14 lines. **One thing that measurement corrected in the brief itself:** in this shape the person is NOT left staring at a failed turn — the model ran, SendMessage delivered its answer into the conversation, and only the checkpoint behind it refused. The demo Titan's shape is the other one, where the refusal came before the answer and every turn ended with nothing. The leg now holds the product to the rule that covers both: a person is never left silent, either an answer landed or the conversation says the store needs repair. **The `--box` leg was also proving less than it claimed and now does not:** a conversation's route is chosen once and cached for the life of the host process, so the marker it wrote AFTER the agent's first turn changed nothing and that turn would have completed anyway. The damage goes on before the first turn now, and the leg asserts the recovery wrote the conversation file and logged it. **grok-bot-local-vm: `--box` 12 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP** — `repaired the conversation store for 648de5ff-…: 0 entries before, 3 after, nothing set aside`, the verb then `already-healthy` with before 3 / after 3, the next turn 2 → 4, one audit row. (5, 6) *The operator doc described a button that rebuilds the conversation and quoted "Repaired, 115 entries kept", a number no repair ever produced* — it is the console gate's STUB relay answering the page, and the console leg's 26 PASS is now labelled as such in this row and in the gate. `docs/BOX-STORE.md` step 3 now says what the verb does (sets an unreadable write-ahead copy aside, reindexes a database that will not open, turns the stuck state off; the NEXT message rebuilds) and quotes the R750's real answer; `docs/OPERATOR-RUNBOOK.md` says the same. And the claim that the verb plus a message unwedged the demo Titan was wrong: re-verified read-only on the R750 today, that agent's ledger holds ONE `transcript_repair` row, `already-healthy`, before 0 / after 0, and the host log line beside it is `nothing to repair in …`; the line after it, `repaired … 0 entries before, 8 after`, is the message's own in-turn recovery. The customer-facing agent is back and it was the host half that did it. The card's own copy carried the same false promise and was corrected with the doc. **Re-measured after the fixes:** `npm test` 2,175 PASS / 0 FAIL (shared tree), `source:typecheck` clean, `build-host.mjs` `validated-clean-source clean=true`, console leg 26 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP. **SHIPPED AND MEASURED ON THE R750, 2026-09-09, bundle `3f6d6bedcd38`** (host-main.cjs 21,748,066 B, sha256 `77299fb5b632428b…`): `sync.sh --no-install` from a clean detached worktree at `3f6d6be`, `updateHostNow` inside the demo box and Jason's box — both `post-swap watch disarmed: host up 60s on 3f6d6bedcd38 (healthy)` — then the relay restarted LAST because `ui/` changed, and the relay container serves the new console code. Richard's box `titanbot-box-wepegxhh3fpvr83bubvz5xm5` was not swapped and nothing was written inside it. **The demo tenant's Titan, through the product, on the new bundle:** backed up first inside the box on the same volume, `/home/box/sand-data/agent-backups/c63fdce4-4fc0-4ea7-8a1b-93657df2c6c5-2026-09-09T22-32-50Z`, **221 MB**, with **121** transcript entries and **5,053** blobs at that moment. `repairAgentTranscript` — the door the console's button calls — answered `{"before":16,"after":16,"quarantined":[],"outcome":"already-healthy","reason":"this conversation store had nothing to repair"}`, which is the truthful answer for an agent that is no longer stuck, and is the answer `docs/BOX-STORE.md` now quotes instead of the stub's 115. Asked *"Titan, one short sentence: are you answering normally after today's update?"* it answered **"Yes, everything's working normally now."** six seconds later. **After:** entries **121 → 123**, blobs **5,053 → 5,063**, the conversation file **17 lines**, and `TranscriptJournalCorruptionError` still **19** in that box's host log — not one more since the first BOX-6b bundle landed. | M |
| JOURNAL-PIN-1 · **filed 2026-09-09, owned; this is the door BOX-6b's damage came through** | `sand_new_transcript_journal` has no entry in `deploy/box-defaults/gates.json` and none in `PRODUCT_GATE_PINS`, so its value on a live box is whatever a remote evaluation returns. It defaults false and its assignment unit is the userID, and it is read per turn at `source/host/host-runner-composition.ts`. **One evaluation returning true, once, at 2026-09-07 23:00:44Z, wrote a `.journal-mode` marker for the demo tenant's Titan, and that marker is permanent** — `selectRoute` reads it before it reads the gate, so the conversation stays on the journal for ever no matter what the gate says afterwards. Every turn on that agent then failed for two days. BOX-6b makes such a conversation heal itself instead of wedging, so the damage is contained; the claim is still unpinned, and a flip tomorrow claims more conversations. **NOT fixed here, and the reason is the ripple rather than the edit.** The edit is one line, but `scripts/verify-cursor-free.mjs` reads `deploy/box-defaults/gates.json` as the list of gates every box must report as `local pin`, so adding a name there makes that gate red on all three live boxes until each tenant's own `/home/box/sand-data/gates.json` is backfilled — and a box picks its pins up at host start, which this wave may not force. Two traps that branch already paid for: hydrate is re-run in place at seven call sites, so the boot's pending card must not be handed to a rebuild, and a rebuild's answer must be adopted when it lands rather than on the next 15 s heartbeat. **Why it is its own row:** carried as a paragraph inside CHAT-LONG-1 it read, on the status page and in the row header, as closed. | M |

Today there are two Slack things and neither is a product: (1) the Slack MCP connector preset (korotovsky slack-mcp-server, in the box, a pasted `xoxp-` user token from an app the customer creates by hand in eleven steps; read-only until more env is set) and (2) the Slack listener, whose events were polled out of Cursor's backend relay and which therefore can never connect on any box we ship | The product shape is one Slack app, ours, named Titanium Bot, created once at api.slack.com: OAuth redirect on the control plane (`api.titanium.bot/slack/oauth`), an "Add to Slack" button on the console's Slack card, and the per-workspace bot token (`xoxb-`) the redirect hands back stored by the control plane for that tenant and pushed into that tenant's box as the `slack` channel credential, never typed by the customer. Events come in through Slack's Events API to a request URL on the control plane, which routes by `team_id` to the tenant's box, the same path mail takes (MAIL-1). Not Socket Mode: that needs the app-level token in every box, which is one shared secret across customers, the thing PROXY-1 exists to end. Unlisted distribution is enough (installs into any workspace without a Marketplace listing). | TENANT-8 · **landed; the backfill could not see the operator's own box, fixed** (2026-09-09) | CURSOR-1 item 5. A new tenant's box was created with no settings of its own, so every gate fell through to the bundled table and the box behaved however that table happened to be rolled out. That is what made three R750 boxes on one bundle behave three ways. The settings existed (`deploy/box-defaults/`, with a README saying what each pin is and why) and nothing called them: `grep -rn writeBoxDefaults cp/` returned nothing. Measured on the R750 2026-09-08: `gates.json` was MISSING from all three of `/data/titanbot/{demo,richard-avery,north-bay-roofing}/volumes/data`, and `sand-host-settings.json` was missing from `north-bay-roofing`. `gates.json` is the file that carries `sand_auto_review: false` and the rest of the CURSOR-1 pins, which is the whole reason the row exists | **Landed.** `writeBoxDefaults(tenantPaths(slug, config).data)` is in the `directories` step of `cp/provision.mjs`: every `.json` in `deploy/box-defaults/` at 0600, **skipping any file already there**, with the written and skipped names in the step's ledger detail and in the dry run's plan preview. Skipping is the point — the step is retried from where it failed and an operator may have changed a switch since. `tests/cp-provision.test.mjs` covers all three cases: a fresh directory gets both files at 0600 with the CURSOR-1 pin in them, a retry leaves an edited `gates.json` byte for byte as it was while still adding the file that was missing, and a checkout with no defaults directory provisions instead of failing. The three existing tenants are backfilled by `node scripts/backfill-box-defaults.mjs` (`--dry-run` first), which adds only what is missing, restarts nothing, recreates nothing (BOX-6) and prints names only **The backfill discovered tenants by listing `/data/titanbot/*/volumes/data`, and Jason's box does not live there.** Measured read-only on the R750 2026-09-08: `/data/titanbot/` holds `_control-plane, demo, north-bay-roofing, richard-avery, titanium`, and `titanium/` contains only `profile/` with no `volumes/data`; `docker inspect titanbot-box-p927bfqm83ioloibamlvyd7g` puts its sand-data on the named volume `titanbot-box-data` at `/data/docker/volumes/titanbot-box-data/_data`. Reading each box's own mount: the demo box and Richard's both have `gates.json` and `sand-host-settings.json`; Jason's has `sand-host-settings.json` and no `gates.json` at all -- so the CURSOR-1 pins, `sand_auto_review: false` among them, are not applied on the operator's own box, which is the exact three-boxes-three-ways condition this row exists to remove. **Fixed 2026-09-09:** `scripts/backfill-box-defaults.mjs` now discovers BOXES first -- `docker ps --filter label=com.titanbot.role=box`, then each one's `/home/box/sand-data` mount `Source` out of `docker inspect`, whether that is a bind of a tenant tree or a named volume -- and keeps the tenant-tree walk as a second source so a provisioned-but-not-running tenant is still covered, folding duplicates by resolved path. A missing `/data/titanbot` is no longer fatal, because a host with running boxes on named volumes and no tenant tree is precisely the host this has to work on | S |
| UX-ERR-1 · **landed** (2026-09-08) | Jason, 2026-09-07 08:04: "I said something to Titan and it popped up like he was talking, then it went away. I don't see any errors." A failed agent run (BOX-6) left the console with the message marked accepted and no reply, no chip, no note. The failure was in the host log only. Measured 2026-09-08 before the fix: `grep -rn "turn-failed|turnFailed|turn_failed" source/ ui/ scripts/` returned zero hits across the whole tree, so there was no entry to draw and nothing to force a failure against | **Landed.** `source/host/extensions/transcript/turn-failed-entry.ts` maps the failure to plain words (`the conversation store needs repair` for BOX-6, `the model is busy right now`, `this conversation got too long for the model`, and so on) and builds one sentence: `<Agent> could not answer this one: <cause>. Try again, or open the host log.` The host writes it as a `turn-failed` entry on the conversation from the `agent run failed` catch in `turn-runtime.ts`, wrapped, because a store that is itself the failure must not turn a failed turn into a crashed host. `gateway-adapter.js` passes the kind through — a filter that dropped an entry kind it did not know is how the line would have been thrown away one function before the renderer — and `app.js` draws one quiet muted line with a warm rule down its left edge, in the transcript rather than a toast, so it is still there tomorrow. No stack, no error class, no detail field to open. The roster card already went to attention with "The last turn failed". `tests/turn-failed-entry.test.mjs` pins the copy, including that a provider's own wording never reaches the page | S |
| MAIL-1 · **landed** (2026-09-07; three branches reconciled into the trunk in fcccb72, 63806ad and 0d1bc06) | Agents had no way to be reached by email and no way to answer one, so anything a customer sent had to be copied into a conversation by hand **Shipped to console.titanium.bot 2026-09-07 00:55 CDT at 09a5014** (relay restart + in-place host swap): deploy gate 56/56, read-only mail gate 16/16, measured from this Mac. The Resend side (domain receiving, MX record, webhook, the two keys in the console card) is Jason's, in progress. | The relay owns the receive side (`ui/mail-edge.mjs`, mounted from `ui/server.mjs` the way the job bus edge is; nothing in the host bundle changed). `POST /hooks/resend` is public and verifies the Svix signature by hand with node:crypto (256 KB cap, 60 a minute per address, 503 with no secret stored, 401 on a bad or stale signature, 200 on everything past it so Resend stops retrying), reads the message back out of Resend, routes it by a hand-written route then agent name then the catch-all then Titan, and delivers it as one `sendPrompt` with `clientNonce: mail:<email_id>`. **Reviewed and fixed 2026-09-07 (this branch):** the email itself now sits between two lines in the prompt with a sentence saying a stranger wrote it and it is not an instruction, and the header lines are collapsed to one line each so a subject cannot forge a `From:`; the address Resend is read at is the relay's own environment (`GROK_BOT_MAIL_API_BASE`, default api.resend.com) and no longer a console setting, because the stored key travels on that request; concurrent copies of one webhook are held by an in-process set as well as the ledger, so ten at once are one delivery; a roster the relay could not read answers 503 with no ledger row instead of 200 no_route, which used to lose the mail for good on a gateway blip; one normalization now makes the published address and matches it, with routes read before the name match and a note on the card when two agents clash; the gate refuses `--stub` unless the target is loopback with no secrets stored; and docs/MAIL.md now tells the operator to make a sending-only key for agents and keep the full-access key on the relay. The card's Receiving switch is a real gate rather than a label: with it off a signed message answers `200 {"ignored":"disabled"}` and nothing is delivered, checked after the signature so a stranger is never told which it was. `GET /mail/settings` and `POST /mail/settings` is the console's half, behind the session, and both secrets are write-only: the shape either route answers carries `apiKeySet` and `webhookSecretSet` and never a value. Settings in `ui/mail.json` (0600, owned like its directory, gitignored), one line per event in `ui/mail-inbox.jsonl` with no body and no secret. An Email card in Settings beside the Job bus card; an `email` seed skill in the bundle so an agent can send and thread a reply from its shell with `$RESEND_API_KEY`. **Three branches built this in parallel and only one of them had a relay to run against, so that one is what shipped**; from the other two, the skill took the exact secret-request to send when the key is missing, what a 401 and a 403 from Resend each mean, and the rule not to email anybody the operator did not ask for, and the card gained the 24 tests it was missing (its five click branches moved into `mailClick` beside the rest of the block to be reachable from one). **Measured on this Mac, 2026-09-07, against a second relay started on 127.0.0.1:7799 with a stub Resend and the same box the running relay uses:** `npm test` 767/767 (including `tests/mail-edge.test.mjs` 21 and `tests/machine-room-mail.test.mjs` 24), both typechecks exit 0, `scripts/verify-mail.mjs --stub` **35 PASS 0 FAIL** with the signed synthetic email delivered to a real agent on the box and its ledger row read back, read-only mode 16 PASS 0 FAIL, and `verify-dashboard --offline` 56 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 not reached. The contract is docs/MAIL.md Operator steps are docs/MAIL.md section 1: verify the domain in Resend, add the MX record at the apex, create the `email.received` webhook, paste the two values into the card, give each agent RESEND_API_KEY through the secure card. Not measured against real Resend: the field names on its inbound `GET /emails/receiving/{id}` are read defensively (every field optional, snake_case and camelCase both accepted) and no leg has yet seen a real inbound message **Switched on for Jason's workspace 2026-09-07 22:05 (Jason: "I would like to switch the email on"), domain myagents.email** (verified in Resend, receiving enabled, MX/SPF/DKIM/DMARC done by Jason earlier tonight; titanium.bot has receiving disabled). Done through the product's own routes: a Resend webhook for email.received at https://console.titanium.bot/hooks/resend, a dedicated full-access key for the relay, and POST /mail/settings with the gateway bearer from inside the operator box (enabled, domain, from name, catch-all Titan). The card reports apiKeySet and webhookSecretSet true and five addresses (titan@, scribe@, instagrammarketer@, xmarketer@, facebookmarketer@myagents.email). **Measured:** a message sent through Resend from probe@myagents.email to titan@myagents.email at 03:06:10Z was in the ledger as delivered to Titan at 03:06:12Z, and Titan's next reply began "Mail's live: titan@myagents.email just received its first message". Titan's sending key (a Resend key scoped to sending on myagents.email only) is the one piece not yet in his shell: setConnectorSecret refuses the reserved shell route, which only the secret card reaches; see the status page for where that landed. | M |

**Regression caught by the final gate pass, fixed before commit.** Giving subagents their own
audit identity created `agents/subagent-<id>/audit.jsonl` beside the real agents, and the roster
enumerates every directory there and *recovers* a missing database into a new agent: seventeen
phantom "New Agent" rows appeared with materialized databases. Root cause underneath: the
reconstruction minted subagent ids as `subagent-<uuid>` while the shared predicate
`isSandSubagentId` expects `sand-subagent-`, so upstream's own guards (roster projection, the
subagent branch of `getConversationOutline`) never matched. Ids now carry the shared prefix, the
three directory enumerations skip subagent ledgers, and `verify-toolset` asserts the roster holds
no subagent id after any subagent run. Side effect worth having: the conversation outline for a
subagent id now answers.

**How the browser subagent was actually broken, four faults deep.** The audit's "gated off" was
the first layer only. With the gate open: (1) the prompt glue was built once with every identity
flag false, so the child got the chief's desktop prompt with no Browser section and a ban on
driving Chrome from Shell; (2) the fifteen browser tools declared no `parameters`, and the
OpenAI-compatible executor silently drops such tools, so on the wire the child held Shell and Read
only, which is why it truthfully said its browser tools were unavailable; (3) the tools kept
Cursor's `execute(context, args, metadata)` order while this core calls `execute(ctx,
interactionHandler, args, meta)`, so every call failed "url is required"; (4) the auto-review
preflight probes Chrome on the agent's display before the driver has launched it, and this box's
shell executor reports the refused connection as `failure` with an exit code, which the preflight
read as a capture error instead of its own chrome-unreachable path. Each layer was found by
instrumenting, not by reading: the `[sand][toolset]` line (what buildTurnTools offered), the new
`[sand][wire]` line (what actually left for the provider), the child's own audit ledger, and a
logged cause in the preflight. Both switches live in `/home/box/sand-data/sand-host-settings.json`
(`SAND_TOOL_TRACE`, `SAND_BROWSER_USE`) and flip on a running box.


**BOX-4, 2026-09-05 09:05 CDT (measured on the Mac box; the R750 was unreachable from 08:55).** The Mac box's "crash loop" was not the host: three pressure CPU profiles show the host process idle, and the container's 666% CPU was the box supervisor's desktop pass respawning seat components that the host had already rebuilt, with its wrapper killing the host every time the storm stalled the heartbeat. Fixed with the image's own switch, `SAND_DESKTOP_SUPERVISION_DISABLED=1`, now in the compose, the hand-run installer and the Mac recreate script; live on the R750 since 09:19 (the server came back from the UPS shutdown already in the storm, sixteen respawns in three minutes; the compose PATCH plus restart ended it, deploy gate green with 0 failing). DISPLAY-7 was this same registry replaying the blank agent's seat and is closed by it. CONNECT-1's fix passed both connector-plane arms on the recreated box (plain and `--model-tool`).


**RESIDUALS, 2026-09-05 11:07 CDT (measured on the Mac box, bundle 7351652 plus the console fix).** Five rows as one workflow: five implementers in isolated worktrees, one integrator that merged, deployed once and ran the gates one after another, a skeptic per row, a fixer per refutation. Every row was refuted once on a real finding (a turn boundary that reset on a subagent's start, a paused routine relabelled as failing, a loading placeholder counted as settled, a gate that could not tell old from new, a red teach gate) and fixed. The regate was green except the plain dashboard gate, which no longer fits 280 s (GATE-1) and whose full run found the console never drawing a routine the host wrote on its own; that was a fourth layer of AUTOMATION-3 and is landed. Final: 401 unit tests, typecheck, and every gate green including the dashboard at 147 checks.

**Job bus wave, building 2026-09-05 22:20 CDT against `docs/JOB-BUS.md` (the contract, written
from Jason's spec in `nextgen-training` 8abeaed).** The Chief of Staff needs a way to hand this
instance a piece of work and get back a result it can trust, without being handed a shell, a
browser, CDP, VNC or the desktop. So: an allowlisted job API on the relay at `/v1`, behind its own
bearer (`TITAN_JOB_TOKEN`) that is never the console session; a store, a queue, an idempotency
key, an append-only audit and one in-host worker on the gateway; and the evidence layer
(`docs/EVIDENCE-CONTRACT.md`) deciding whether a worker's claimed commits and artifacts are
supported by the receipts of the tools it actually ran. A job that claims a commit no receipt
covers fails as `attestation did not hold` with the unsupported claims attached; the model never
decides its own verdict. Two job types and no more: `health.ping` and `nextgen.chapter`. Rows
JOBBUS-1..5 below; the pieces were built in parallel to that one file, which is why every route,
status code, command name, settings name and record field in it is binding.

**§10, the hardening pass, was written after a contract skeptic read sections 1 to 9 and is being
built in the same three lanes those sections split along: host, relay, and console with the gates
and the docs.** It wins wherever it and the sections above it disagree, and sections 1 to 9 now
carry a one-line pointer at each heading saying what it takes back. The four changes that decide
whether shipping this code opens a door by itself: the bus is disabled until the operator turns it
on (§10.7), `/v1` answers a uniform `401` so an unconfigured deployment cannot be told from a wrong
key (§10.6), the worker runs in a per-job clone with its connectors stripped rather than in the
mapped agent's own conversation (§10.2), and `done` needs GitHub itself to confirm every commit and
file the reply claimed, not just this host's receipts (§10.4).

| Id | Finding | Next | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| JOBBUS-1 | Nothing on this instance accepts work from another machine. The Chief of Staff can only reach an agent the way a person does, through the console or a chat listener, and it gets back prose it has to believe | The gateway half in `source/host/extensions/job-bus/`: the store on the box data volume (`job-bus/jobs.json` written atomically, the last 500 terminal jobs kept plus `retired` idempotency keys; `job-bus/audit.jsonl` append-only and chained by `prev`; `job-bus/settings.json` behind `jobBusGetSettings` / `jobBusSetSettings`), the type allowlist, the §10.1 payload validation and repos allowlist, idempotency, `jobBusHealth` / `jobBusCreate` / `jobBusGet` / `jobBusCancel` / `jobBusArtifacts` / `jobBusList` registered in `gateway-protocol.ts`, and one in-host worker that clones the mapped agent per job, sends §6's prompt into the clone, attests the reply with `readAgentEvidence` and against GitHub, and deletes the clone. **Hardened per §10 in `efa13c1`, integrated here** (host lane) **Merged to the trunk in `304670c` and gated on the Mac box 2026-09-06: `scripts/verify-job-bus.mjs` 58/58 PASS against bundle `bb3896f4` (identity checked, not remembered: `scripts/bundle-identity.sh` says built, deployed and in-box are all `bb3896f4`, log `c2fix-bundle-identity.log`).** | L |
| JOBBUS-2 | The relay's only door is the console session, so there is no way to let a machine in without also letting it hold the whole gateway surface | The `/v1` edge in `ui/server.mjs` and `ui/job-bus-edge.mjs`, handled BEFORE the console auth gate and never accepting a console cookie: a uniform `401` with `www-authenticate` whether the token is unconfigured, missing or wrong, `413` over 64 KB, `429` over 120 a minute per client and 600 a minute across the bus, failed bearers counting against the same lockout the login uses, `404`/`405`, the secret-shaped payload refusal, header-over-body idempotency, `submitter: "cos"` plus `client` and `submitter_id` added by the relay. The token resolves env, then `<profile>/job-bus.json` at 0600, then closed, compared in constant time. The four console routes that generate, set and clear it. **Hardened per §10 in `312ef89`, integrated here** (relay lane) **Merged to the trunk in `304670c` and gated on the Mac box 2026-09-06: `scripts/verify-job-bus.mjs` 58/58 PASS against bundle `bb3896f4` (identity checked, not remembered: `scripts/bundle-identity.sh` says built, deployed and in-box are all `bb3896f4`, log `c2fix-bundle-identity.log`).** | M |
| JOBBUS-3 | A bus with no surface is a bus nobody can see: no way to mint a token, no way to know whether one is set or where it came from, no way to turn it on or off, no way to say which agent runs a job type or which repositories it may touch, and no way to see what the CoS has been asking for or which job is stuck on a human | Settings → Job bus, reading and writing `jobBusGetSettings` / `jobBusSetSettings` rather than the retired `SAND_JOB_BUS_*` names: the Enabled switch (default off, armed by generating or setting a token), the configured state and its source, the base URL, Generate / Set / Clear with the token shown once beside its warning, a curl example carrying a placeholder, the worker mapping as a job type against an agent picked off the roster and stored by id, the repos allowlist, the connectors the per-job clone keeps, the two timeouts and `maxOpen`, and the jobs table naming the clone and the needs-human reason, refreshed on the host's `job-bus` event. Deploy: `TITAN_JOB_TOKEN` through the Coolify compose (no `:-`) and the R750 relay, with the operator steps in both READMEs and the runbook. **Hardened per §10 in `a4a4431`, integrated here** (console, gates and docs lane) **Merged to the trunk in `304670c` and gated on the Mac box 2026-09-06: `scripts/verify-job-bus.mjs` 58/58 PASS against bundle `bb3896f4` (identity checked, not remembered: `scripts/bundle-identity.sh` says built, deployed and in-box are all `bb3896f4`, log `c2fix-bundle-identity.log`).** | M |

Open until he runs it | S |
| JOBBUS-5 | § 10.4's second layer is still the one part of the contract nothing here can exercise: the out-of-band GitHub check needs the box's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` and a real repository, so commit-exists, branch-contains, `size == bytes`, `sha256(content) == sha256` and the `PLACEHOLDER_LOAD_FROM_DISK` refusal are code with no run behind them, and a bug in any of them fails open into `done` rather than closed. **Closed by the 10.9 pass (2026-09-06):** the residual conditions are no longer only in a reviewer's head — §10.9 "Deviations and residuals" now lists all of them in the contract itself (the settings guard, `maxOpen` left hot, `allowUnattested`, the quarantine, the arming paths, the shared-user residual, the box-wide `GITHUB_TOKEN`, the static bearer). That pass also closed, with a test each: `policy.require_attestation:false` is now the operator's switch (`allowUnattested`, default false) rather than any bearer holder's, so a caller can no longer have the bus mark a job done on the model's unchecked word; the store verifies `jobs.json`, its audit chain and `settings.json` at start and quarantines what it cannot believe, with the bus off and a `store_quarantined` row, and catches an `audit.jsonl` rewritten whole by the byte length the host remembers — condition (c) is now detected within a host's lifetime, though not anchored off the box; persistence errors fail the transition and write `store_unwritable` instead of being swallowed; the per-job clone loses the automations it inherited; `tick()` is re-entrant-guarded; a missing worker is retried twice before `needs_human`; turning the bus off no longer strands a running job or its clone; an unreadable transcript fails at once; `branch`, `rules_ref` and `repo` can no longer begin with `-`; the relay arms the bus on its own start for the `TITAN_JOB_TOKEN` deploy path; `safeEqual` no longer leaks the configured token's length; and the box gate stopped accepting a 404 as a refusal or either audit-hashing convention. **Merged to the trunk in `304670c` and gated on the Mac box 2026-09-06: `scripts/verify-job-bus.mjs` 58/58 PASS against bundle `bb3896f4` (identity checked, not remembered: `scripts/bundle-identity.sh` says built, deployed and in-box are all `bb3896f4`, log `c2fix-bundle-identity.log`), its first run ever.** Conditions (a) the static shared bearer and (b) the clone being isolated at the connector level only are unchanged and now written down. **Second review (2026-09-06):** the quarantine was not sticky (one restart cleared it and, with `TITAN_JOB_TOKEN` set, re-armed the bus over a tampered `audit.jsonl`), and the chain was verified only on the path where `jobs.json` parsed, which is not the shape a quarantine leaves behind. The chain is now verified unconditionally, a break moves `audit.jsonl` aside and opens a FRESH chain instead of appending the receipt to the file that failed, and the decision is written to `quarantine.json` and re-read at start. `jobs.json` is now checked against that chain (`status`, `payload_sha256` and `type idempotency_key` per record, and the `retired` map too), not only against a `payload_sha256` a tamperer can recompute. The queue clock stops with the bus, so maintenance no longer fails every queued job as "queued too long". Terminal transitions dispose the clone in a `finally`, so an unwritable cache no longer leaks it. And the claim that the clone's connectors "are listed the way production actually sees them" is withdrawn: `cloneAgentDir` copies no channels directory, so the list is empty for every clone this tree can make and the strip loop is dead code kept for the day one is copied. §10.9 says so now. | Prove layer 2 on the R750 with one real `nextgen.chapter` job against `webdevtodayjason/nextgen-training`, BOTH ways: a job whose commits and files are really there reaching `done`, and a doctored result whose sha is not on the branch failing with `commit:<sha>` unsupported. That merges with JOBBUS-4's smoke rather than preceding it. Then one decision with Jason per remaining condition: a per-caller credential (mTLS or a signed short-lived token) for (a); a tool-stripped clone, which is a host change and not a settings one, for (b); the audit head mirrored off the box, or the chain signed with a key the worker cannot reach, to finish (c). None is a v1 blocker; all three are v2 candidates | M |
| JOBBUS-6 | The second review's remaining lows, carried forward unfixed from the `304670c` merge and the 2026-09-06 Mac-box gate: `settings.json` is only checked at host start; the gate's integrity leg is a tautology; the `no_worker` retry never waits its last interval; the `noWorkerSince` map leaks on cancel or expire; inherited automations are cleared after the clone exists | Take them one at a time, each with a test or a gate line: re-check `settings.json` when it is reloaded rather than only at start; make the integrity leg assert against a state it did not itself produce; give the last `no_worker` retry its interval before `needs_human`; drop the `noWorkerSince` entry on every terminal transition, not only on dispatch; clear the inherited automations before the clone can run rather than after it exists. Owner: the next job-bus pass, next action: the `settings.json` reload check, which is the only one of the five that can change what the bus does at runtime | M |
**Wave BROWSER is in flight (2026-09-07). Titan gets a browser back.** Jason, 18:27: *"Titan
should be able to use a browser. If that's been taken away from Titan, it needs to be given back as
a tool."* It had been. Every box has run Chrome the whole time, on the person's own profile, on the
screen the console's desktop view shows, with a debug port already open on loopback, and the main
agent was offered none of it, because one predicate in `turn-toolset.ts` handed the browser tools
only to a subagent behind a gate that is off. Asked to read a page, Titan answered in prose about
what it probably said. The shape of the fix: four tools in his own hands for the common case
(`browser_open`, `browser_click`, `browser_type`, `browser_screenshot`), the desktop subagent kept
for long multi-step jobs, and the person never sees a tool name. Titan says what he found. The
whole design is [docs/BROWSER.md](BROWSER.md).

Eight results, **eight image parts in the turn history and eight in the request that left**, every one `image/jpeg` at **1280x656** (example.com 14,764 bytes, the YouTube channel 70,204). Five opens, **five `browser_navigation` rows** in the audit ledger, every row with its url and its title. Four other things were wrong and are fixed: the tool factory stamped `image/png` on a JPEG; a tab whose renderer wedged failed every later action instead of being replaced (measured: after a YouTube page, `Page.enable` would not answer in 10 s, so steps 3-8 all failed); a shell failure reached the model as "Browser driver shell failed (failure)", a sentence with no fact in it; and the driver could be killed by the shell's own budget before it could say anything, so it now answers inside 55 s in plain words. **Costs six minutes against a box** -- eight real page loads -- so it does not fit a 290 s budget; run it detached.
